# Llama-3.1-8B attention optimization on MI455X (gfx1250): progress summary (as of 2026-10-07)

- **Scope**: the attention used in Llama-3.1-8B training. Op shape b4 s8192 hq32 hkv8 d128 bf16, causal (bottom-right; equivalent to top-left when Sq=Skv), BSHD, GQA 4. The e2e setup is Llama-3.1-8B with 32 layers (some 09-15/16 experiments used 8 layers), BF16, single card, MBS=GBS=4, seq 8192 (32,768 tokens per step), AC none, torch.compile off.
- **FLOP convention**: the current convention is op-evolve's `tools/op_flops.py`: per call, fwd 2.199292e12 and bwd 5.498229e12 (5-GEMM nominal count), fwd+bwd 7.697e12. The 09-13..09-16 documents report only the fwd+bwd total (7.697e12 ÷ total time); this report does not mix in other conventions such as the 7-GEMM issued count or the old harness's fwd ≈2.233e12; see Appendix A.3.
- **Data cutoff**: the last measurement is the product-branch e2e tutorial of 2026-10-05 ((d) also records a wedge in another project on 10-06, as a risk reference only). This report was written on 2026-10-07 and revised once the same day after a completeness review (the pre-review full text is kept in `_work/SUMMARY.v1.md`).
- **Disclaimer**: this report only compiles existing records (the daily reports under `output/`, the op-evolve ledgers, session logs, git history); no GPU job was run for it. A0's GPU is currently in use by a colleague.
- **Files** (all under `Primus-Turbo/output/1007__summary/`):
  - `SUMMARY.en.md`: this report (English version), final; where it disagrees with any English file below, SUMMARY.en.md prevails.
  - `rounds.en.xlsx`: per-round details, one row per round (198 rows, none merged). Sheet 1 "All rounds" holds all the details, sheet 2 "Improvement rounds" separately lists the rounds that brought an improvement and the size of each improvement, and sheet 3 "Legend" explains the columns, the machine codes and the colors. The data source is `parts/en/rounds.en.csv`.
  - `REPORT-1007.en.html`: the HTML version of this report, self-contained; just open it in a browser.
  - Chinese version: `SUMMARY.md`, `REPORT-1007.html`, `rounds.xlsx` (the originals), which correspond one-to-one with the English version (the English sources are in `parts/en/`); where the two disagree, the Chinese version is authoritative.
  - `parts/`: section drafts. SUMMARY.md is the direct concatenation, in this order, of `0_head.md` (title, notes, one-page conclusions, timeline), `a_mainline.md`, `b_backends.md`, `c_rounds.md`, `d_problems.md` and `z_appendix.md` (Appendix A/B/C); `rounds.csv` is the data source of rounds.xlsx (including columns such as stage, whether improved, and improvement size); `en/` holds the corresponding English source files. On content, SUMMARY.md prevails.
  - `_work/`: scratch area: session text exports (`sessions/`), intermediate files and pre-edit backups of each subtask (`scratch/`), the v1 section drafts (`v1/`), the pre-review full text `SUMMARY.v1.md`, and the session-export scripts (`tools/`). It can be deleted as a whole without affecting the files above (`_work/.gitignore` already ignores all of its contents).
  - `tools/`: `build.sh` concatenates `parts/` into SUMMARY.md / SUMMARY.en.md and generates the two HTML files and the two xlsx files (CPU only); `md2html.py` is the HTML conversion script and `build_xlsx.py` generates the xlsx files. After editing `parts/`, just run `tools/build.sh`.
- **Notation**: A0 = `heliosr-1b114-c07-1` (this machine, single card); B0 = `ctheliosp-1b112-a37-1` (4 cards). Before its 09-29 firmware reflash, A0 was under a VR throttle (sclk capped at 1100 MHz); **A0's absolute numbers from before 09-29 can be compared neither with later A0 numbers nor with B0**. The four sections use different names for the same clock phase (A0-VR / E1 / A0-T / A0-throttled, etc.); see Appendix A.1 for the mapping. All times are UTC.

## 0. One-page conclusions

1. **Starting point: unmodified main does not run on gfx1250 by default.** On upstream main `c1325c7e` (#481), with torch 2.11 it crashes on import (`register_opaque_type`); default dispatch lands on CK, which supports only CDNA (without aiter in the image, the very first step hits ImportError; with aiter installed, bwd fails mid-training with `invalid argument for fmha_bwd`); all tests on gfx1250 are skipped. The only backend that can be pinned by hand, Triton, copies CDNA's single config: on A0-VR, fwd+bwd 58.424 ms / 131.7 TF/s (bench0910, total convention; 59.642 ms / 129.0 on the branch tree's tune ruler), ~1.9× slower than flex, with the dkdv kernel alone taking 61.5%. On the e2e side, A0 had never completed a single step before; once it ran on 09-13 it reached only 245 tok/s, 133.7 s/step (GEMM fell back to rocBLAS, 27.4 TF/s), and `converters: []` meant that turbo attention never even entered e2e. Details in (a).
2. **Backends after adaptation (compared only within the same clock phase).** The A0-VR 09-13 bake-off (fwd+bwd total, fastest first): aiter Triton with 2 knobs 31.207 ms, flex 31.337, aiter Triton factory default 34.565, turbo Triton tuned 36.405, turbo Triton factory default 59.642, SDPA FLASH 97.063. The best Triton-family bwd takes about 2–2.4 times as long as ASM (≈, across rulers: the Triton figure is a reading from E1 `tune_attention.py`, whose timing includes the autograd plumbing; ASM is taken as the E3 benchmark.py bar of 7.68 ms, or as the slower same-process bring-up reading of 8.661 ms), and the best fwd about 1.5 times (≈, B0 09-14); this is a structural gap (a single ASM WG uses the full 320 KiB of LDS and 1024 VGPRs). aiter's precompiled ASM is the fastest ready-made implementation: on A0-VR, fwd 1.5724 ms / 1398.67 TF/s and bwd 7.6766 ms / ≈716 TF/s (09-24 census); after A0's firmware reflash, fwd 1.257 ms / 1750 and bwd 5.503 ms / 999 TF/s (09-30, blocked ruler, randn). Details in (b).
3. **The fastest implementation, ASM, cannot be used as is.** bwd has no Python entry point for gfx1250 (`can_impl_fmha_v3_bwd` does not recognize gfx1250), so a ~674-line launcher had to be written by hand; under GQA it writes dk/dv out of bounds per q head (at ratio=4, dk −0.94 / dv −0.65 dB); the workaround is `dkdv_heads=q` plus a host-side reduction, which costs 1.254 GiB more peak memory and 0.482 ms more in backward (A0-VR, +5.2%); only 52 `.co` files were released for gfx1250 (gfx950 has 1466), only 6 of them bwd, and no varlen; dq uses fp32 atomic accumulation, so results are nondeterministic; one call takes 3 kernels and 6 dispatches; aiter pins flydsl 0.3.2 and Primus-Turbo pins 0.2.4, so the two cannot share a process and ASM cannot become an upstream product backend. In addition, the ASM bwd 10.160 ms / 541 TF/s measured on 09-15 was an artifact of the autograd shim and was not retracted until 09-24; until then, FlyDSL's progress had been reported as 0.92× of ASM when it was actually 0.70–0.72×. These problems are the reasons for the 09-17 decision to develop in-house FlyDSL kernels.
4. **FlyDSL bwd: from 1/12.6 of ASM to 103.8% (the two endpoints lie in different firmware phases).** Hand-written from scratch on 09-17; on A0-VR, r0 ran at 96.05 ms / 57.24 TF/s (1/12.6 of same-session ASM); after 20 op-evolve rounds on A0-VR, r20 reached 511.42 TF/s (10.75 ms, ~0.71–0.72× of ASM); on B0, r29 (= r19h + u2n) reached 656.5 TF/s, 80.4% of ASM. The same r29 code on the same blocked ruler was only 69.5% of ASM on A0-VR (09-28), but 83.6% on A0 after the firmware reflash (09-30): estimated from r29, about 14 percentage points of the headline progress come from the reflash (FlyDSL is more clock-sensitive than ASM), not from the code. The 09-30 hand campaign s1–s6, built around a 3-stage TDM LDS ring plus reading the next iteration's B operand back into registers ahead of time, brought bwd to **5.295–5.300 ms / ≈1037–1038 TF/s, 103.8% of ASM (5.497–5.500 ms)** (A0 after the reflash, blocked ruler, randn, same process, bitwise deterministic). A 10-02 re-check with real data gave an s6/ASM time ratio of 0.972 on the blk ruler and 1.038 on the gb ruler at the training operating point. s6 still issues 7 GEMMs, doing ~39% more matrix math than ASM.
5. **FlyDSL fwd: still ~8% slower, and 35% slower at the training operating point.** The A0-VR fwd job went from 935.04 TF/s at r0 to 1111.15 TF/s at r11 (+18.8%; 78.9% of the same-session beat of 1407.8 TF/s, 79.4% against the bar of 1398.67 TF/s). On B0, speculative softmax was 4.6% faster on randn but actually slower with real data at the training clock; r16 turns speculation off (i.e., r13ns) and is 12–23% faster with real data plus the training clock. The current fwd champion is r16 = r13ns: on A0 after the reflash, measured on 09-30 at 1.353 ms / 1626 TF/s vs ASM 1.257 ms, a time ratio of 1.076 (92.9% of ASM); with real data on 10-02, blk 1.081 and gb **1.349**. fwd is now the main remaining gap.
6. **e2e: FlyDSL is now on par with ASM.** A0, 10-02 (driver 7.1.0-2412954, 32 layers, nkfix, `NKFIX_CHECK=1`, three arms alternating in the same process, median training sclk ~1.50 GHz): fwd r16 + bwd s6 at 1,349.4 / 1,349.9 ms per step and 24,283 / 24,275 tok/s, **0.999** of ASM (1,350.6 ms, 24,262 tok/s); fwd r16 + bwd r29 at 1.027. Split with CUDA events (kineto traces are invalid on A0): attention bwd is ~4 ms per step faster than ASM (adjacent-pair difference 4.5 ms; difference between the two arms' medians 3.8–4.4 ms), fwd is ~9.2 ms slower, so attention as a whole costs ~4.3–5.2 ms more, yet the step is 0.7–2.0 ms faster; this ~6 ms discrepancy (step − FA is −6.3 ms in both processes) lies in the parts outside attention or in timing noise and has not been broken down. Versus r29, s6 saves ~53 ms of attention bwd per step and the step is 37–39 ms faster, again with ~14–16 ms not broken down. Before that, the final B0 09-28 r16 + r29 stood at 1.0323 / 1.0328 (20,091 vs 20,747 tok/s). The product branch (r16 + r29, flydsl 0.2.4) measured 1,361.9 ms/step and 24,061 tok/s in the 10-05 tutorial, but that was with `NKFIX_CHECK=0`, so it cannot be compared directly with the `NKFIX_CHECK=1` ASM 1,350.6 ms above (a direct comparison would make it look only 0.8% slower): correcting by the ~+33 ms per step of `NKFIX_CHECK=1` (measured on B0), the branch is ~3% slower than ASM (estimated, not measured), consistent with the 1.027 of the flyr29 arm, which is also r16 + r29.
7. **The top e2e lever is actually GEMM (hipBLASLt).** The first problem was a misplaced library path: 402 files sat in a `library/gfx1250/` subdirectory; after setting `HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH`, A0-VR at 32 layers went from 244 to 2,027 tok/s (8.3×). The second problem was that the NN (dgrad) and wgrad layouts lacked pure-bf16 tuned libraries and fell back to the GEMV tile `MT32x16x32`, at only 50–80 TF/s, with GEMM taking 94–96% of the step. nkfix (rewriting the operand layout of `aten::mm` with `TorchDispatchMode` so that it hits `MT256x256x128`) took A0-VR at 8 layers from 6,128 to 37,746 tok/s (6.16×) and the B0 32-layer ASM arm from 2,016–2,022 to 20,718 tok/s (10.3×). Only once GEMM was fixed did ASM/FlyDSL attention account for ~15–22% of the step, so that op-level improvements became visible in e2e. The cost: 21% of the nkfix runs on A0 produced NaN (8/39 vs 0/35, p=0.004), so numbers such as 49,878, 11,608/5.85×, 38,043/6.21× and +14.40% were retracted; the B0 rewrite ran 8 times, 486 steps, with 0 NaNs. `TORCH_BLAS_PREFER_HIPBLASLT` is silently overridden in at least three places; the host hipBLASLt libraries are associated with several NaN and wedge incidents, so since 10-02 (h85) only the image's libraries are used.
8. **Wedges and AC power cycles are the largest time cost.** Over 09-11..10-02, A0 went through 27 manual power cycles or reboots because of wedges ((d) d.1.4, deduplicated across sources; 25 if the two on 09-11 were warm reboots; 28 counting the ineffective AC cycle during the 09-29 driver/firmware mismatch), 9 of them on 09-16 alone; 82% of the wall-clock time of the five rounds on 09-21..22 went to wedges and waiting for AC. By the first dmesg line they fall into three classes: class A is the on-card fp32 Tensile reference GEMM going out of bounds, class B starts with an `INVALIDATE_TLBS` timeout, and class C is a fault storm set off by an out-of-bounds read in a candidate kernel. All four precursor discriminants proposed over time were falsified; the conclusion is that there is no usable predictor, so alerts are raised only on the wedge itself. The triggers actually located and eliminated: the on-card fp32 reference GEMM (refcache, h50), running several shapes in one process (now one shape per process), autotune inside training steps (now an offline table), inductor autotune caused by `converters: []`, PC sampling (permanently disabled), new kernels going straight to prod, and the host hipBLASLt libraries (h85: toy first, then prod; compile-only for reading VGPRs; image libraries only). Also, risk is priced per startup (startup-phase failures on 09-16: 3/14, i.e., 21%). Power cuts also truncated git objects twice (6 and 255 objects), which is where the rule "push at the end of every round" comes from.
9. **The rulers and criteria themselves rewrote the conclusions several times.** Per-call interleaved timing systematically biased the FlyDSL/ASM ratio low (fwd by ~25%, bwd by ~3.4%); after switching to blocked timing, two false wins were retracted; conclusions can be opposite between randn and real data, and between the op clock and the training operating point (~1.28 GHz after GEMM bursts); the framework's criteria went wrong three times (A0 r15 promoted on an arithmetic mean, B0 r27 misjudged as target_met by geomean, A0 r24 a false positive from the fast median), each corrected by hand. After A0's 09-29 firmware reflash (VBIOS 630A→700E, SMU 125.7.1→125.12.0, with the driver changed at the same time from 7.1.1-2397345 to 7.1.0-2411946), the same code ran 1.24–1.67× faster at op level, and all A0 absolute numbers from before 09-29 are void. The e2e 1.44× (1,947→1,351 ms) cited by the tutorial is not purely the effect of the reflash: the 1,947 ms comes from the 09-28 batch of runs that was later voided as a whole (a0_p3a); when that process first loaded the FlyDSL fwd tree at step 2, the tree's `_env.py` repointed `HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH` to the host library (whether the subsequent GEMMs were affected is unproven); the 1,351 ms was measured on 10-02, and in between the driver was changed again on 10-01, to 7.1.0-2412954.
10. **Unfinished work and next steps.** ① fwd: still ~8% slower at op level, 1.349× at the training operating point, ~9 ms per step in e2e. ② s6 has not yet been ported into the product branch `dev/lhz/llama31_attn_opt` (the branch has r29, 1.235× of ASM at op level); the PR has not been opened yet, and the 1.032 cited in `PR_BODY.md` is an old cross-machine number from B0 09-28 that needs updating. ③ The gb scoring ruler for the training operating point is designed but not installed; the fwd job has been restored from the B0 backup but not launched; both need user approval. ④ The 5-GEMM fused version w4f is limited by whole-chip atomic throughput and is paused. ⑤ Unexplained phenomena: in e2e, ASM is faster than either op ruler measured it (suspected: the inputs are hot in MALL; not measured); the causes of class-B wedges and of startup-phase MES faults; the root cause of the nkfix NaNs on A0. Several skill documents also need correcting; see the end of section (d).

## (a) Before optimization: what goes wrong when running the main branch as-is

Scope: the start-up phase, 2026-09-10..09-14. In this section "main" means the head of upstream main at the time, `c1325c7e` (#481, 2026-09-09, the first time a Triton dense attention backend was added for gfx1250). The first working branch, `gfx1250-attn-dispatch-and-tuning`, was branched off it (`git merge-base f8c45dee origin/main` = `c1325c7e`). The "current state" column in the tables has been checked against the local `origin/main` = `8cda13c7` (09-30). The target op is Llama-3.1-8B attention: b4 s8192 hq32 hkv8 d128 bf16 causal (bottom-right), BSHD, GQA G=4.

Notation and conventions:
- **A0-VR**: A0 (`heliosr-1b114-c07-1`) in its VR-throttled state before the 09-29 firmware reflash; sclk capped at 1100 MHz, ~0.97-1.07 GHz under load. **A0-RF**: after the 09-29 firmware reflash; ~1.74-2.03 GHz at op level, ~1.5 GHz during training. **B0**: `ctheliosp-1b112-a37-1`, 2133-2244 MHz under load. Absolute numbers from the three states are not comparable with one another, and the various "ratios" cannot stand in for one another either: ① Cross-machine A0-VR→B0: 5 like-for-like rows of the same Triton-family code give 1.88-2.01x (median 1.93x; all 7 rows of FINAL-TABLE give 1.88-2.12x, median 1.97x). This is a ratio between two cards, and B0's own dmesg also shows VR throttle warnings (the 0914 repro estimates their cost at ~9%), so it is not A0's own throttle cost (0914__repro__c07/RESULTS.md:32-38, 124-130). ② For ASM attention, also across machines, only the fwd ratio of 1.118x holds (A0-VR 09-15 1.572 ms vs B0 09-14 quiet window 1.406 ms, both ASM fwd on the tune ruler). The claim in 0915__opt/JIRA-TRACE-ANALYSIS.md:148-156 that "attention differs by only 1.12-1.15x across machines" (bwd 1.150x; total 11.726 vs 10.236 ms, 1.146x) does not hold: the bwd item compares A0's shim artifact at 10.160 ms (retracted 09-24) with B0's fused Triton bwd at 8.835 ms (on 09-14 B0 had no ASM bwd yet; `--impl asm` meant ASM forward + fused backward), which are not the same kernel. With fused Triton bwd on both sides, bwd is 17.735/8.835 ≈ 2.01x and the total is 19.285/10.235 = 1.88x (0915__repro__c07/RESULTS.md:17; 0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:102-103; 0914__campaign/HANDOFF.md:246; (d) d.3). ③ A0 itself, before→after the firmware reflash: 1.24-1.67x at op level with the same code and the same method (mean of the two orders). The e2e 1.44x is not a clean same-method comparison and is not purely a platform difference: the before value comes from the voided 09-28 run, a process in which the FlyDSL fwd tree repointed `HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH` to the host library midway; between the two runs the driver and the e2e suite both changed as well. It serves only as a reference (see the notes after the a.1 table).
- FLOP convention: fwd 2.199292e12, bwd 5.498229e12, fwd+bwd 7.697e12 / call. The documents of the time reported only the fwd+bwd total, and that total is exactly the sum of the two. The "per-direction TF/s (converted)" column in the tables was computed in this section with the current convention.
- Rulers: **tune** = `tools/gfx1250/tune_attention.py` (median of CUDA events, 20 iters / 5 warmup, 256 MiB L2 flush per rep, SQNR against an fp32 reference computed separately for each of the four tensors out/dq/dk/dv). **bench0910** = the 09-10 log of the Primus-side attention bench (`Primus:output/0910__fa_opt/attn_bench_gfx1250.log`). **E2** = `E2_bench.py` from op-evolve phase-1 (warmup 5, iters 20, repeated 3 times). **bat** = `benchmark/ops/training/bench_attention_turbo.py` (09-30).
- Source paths: relative paths without a prefix are all under `Primus-Turbo/output/`. `0913/` = `0913__opt_plan__claude/`; `ph1/` = `op-evolve:output/0911__fa_gfx1250_phase1/` (op-evolve = `/home/lihuzhan/code/2026_0910__op-evolve/op-evolve/`); `Primus:` = `/home/lihuzhan/code/2026_0828__primus/Primus/`; `wt-llama31 README` = `wt-llama31/docs/gfx1250_llama31_8b_e2e/README.md` (the 10-05 tutorial).

| Clock (the most important variable) | VR throttle. Every amdgpu init prints `WARN: GPU is throttled, expect performance decrease. VR.`. The sclk DPM has only two levels left, 500 / 1100 MHz; MAX_CLK = 1100. On 09-04 the same card could still run 1699-1703 MHz under load (cap 2400). The throttle first appears at the 09-10 22:38 boot, and later reboots did not clear it. Measured under load: attention 1067 MHz / 1227 W (only 49% of the power used), Triton GEMM 1008 MHz / 1770 W, which shows the card is pinned by its clock rather than held back by the power limit | The "throttle cost" has three conventions; they do not measure the same thing and cannot stand in for one another: ① on 09-13, estimated from the clock ratio (1699-1703 MHz on 09-04 vs the 1100 MHz cap) at ~1.65x (the original gives no formula; computing from these two numbers gives ~1.55x); ② the 09-14 document obtained 1.88-2.01x (median 1.93x) from a 5-row same-code comparison on B0 and wrote it as "1.65x → ~1.95x", but that is the A0-VR→B0 cross-card ratio (B0 itself also has VR warnings, at a cost of ~9%), not A0's own throttle cost; ③ same-code comparison on A0 itself, before→after the firmware reflash: 1.24-1.67x at op level (mean of the two orders); the e2e 1.44x is not purely a platform difference, as it also mixes in differences in the BLAS library environment and in the e2e suite, and the driver was changed one more time (see the table below and the notes after it). The first two were unrelated to this project: 09-04 was HipKittens GEMM; 09-06/07 was a grouped-GEMM job, with `SMU: No response` 857 times. The two on 09-11 were triggered by us: E3 evaluated the full 8192² mask_mod on the GPU → GPU Hang; E4 used rocprofv3 PC sampling → memory fault → MES wedged for good. The 5th was on 09-13 at about 13:30 UTC; a 4-worker queue was running at the time and in-flight kernels were repeatedly interrupted with `pkill -9`, which is suspected to be the trigger. In the `extended-v2` image hipBLASLt runs once `HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH` is set, but every layout gets only 60-92 TF/s. Of the 27 requested, only 8 were available, with no counters in the byte, matrix-unit, stall or occupancy categories. When unknown counters are mixed in, it exits 0 but the corresponding columns are silently dropped. Every PC sampling attempt triggered a GPU fault (E4 recorded 2, the 09-13 document records 3); one of them caused a wedge, after which it was disabled across the board | ATT became usable after the 09-29 firmware reflash | ph1/E4.md Headline, §6; 0913/phase0/PLATFORM-ESCALATION.md |

**Same-code comparison on A0 itself before and after the firmware reflash** (A0's own throttle cost. For the op rows the commands, arm directories and blocked ruler are all unchanged and only the platform changed: the firmware, together with the driver swapped during the same period; the two cannot be separated (REPORT §6.3). The e2e row is an exception, see the notes after the table. This is late-stage optimization code, listed here only to give this ratio):

| Measurement (op: b4 s8192 hq32 hkv8 d128 causal, prod, ms in the two orders o1 / o2; e2e: Llama-3.1-8B 32L) | Before: A0-VR (09-28, sclk under load 1010-1060 MHz, driver 7.1.1-2397345) | After: A0-RF (op 09-30, under load 1740-2030 MHz, driver 7.1.0-2411946; e2e 10-02, driver since changed to 7.1.0-2412954) | Ratio (op rows: mean of the two orders; o1 / o2 in parentheses) | Source |
|---|--:|--:|--:|---|
| FlyDSL fwd (r13ns, op) | 2.023 / 1.992 ms | 1.353 / 1.356 ms | 1.48x (1.50 / 1.47) | 0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md §6.1, §6.3; wt-llama31 README §7 takes only o1 and writes 1.50x |
| aiter ASM fwd (op) | 1.564 / 1.543 ms | 1.257 / 1.253 ms | 1.24x (1.24 / 1.23) | 0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md §6.1, §6.3; README §7 |
| FlyDSL bwd r29 (op) | 11.010 / 11.015 ms | 6.583 / 6.596 ms | 1.67x (1.67 / 1.67) | 0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md §6.2; README §7 |
| aiter ASM bwd (op) | 7.654 / 7.666 ms | 5.503 / 5.494 ms | 1.39x (1.39 / 1.40) | Same as above |
| e2e per step, ASM attention + nkfix (`NKFIX_CHECK=1`) | 1,947 ms (09-28 a0_p3a, from the voided batch of runs, see below) | 1,351 ms (1350.6; 10-02, new e2e suite, median sclk during training ~1.50 GHz) | 1.44x (not purely a platform difference, see below) | 0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md §2, §5.3, §6.4; 1002__e2e/RESULT-e2e.md, E2E-PLAN.md §1-§3; README §7 |

The clock ratio under load is ~1.75x, and every measured speedup is below it: FlyDSL is more sensitive to sclk, while ASM fwd barely changes with sclk. In the same section the other FlyDSL fwd arms give 1.45-1.69x per order (0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md §6.1, §6.3). The FlyDSL fwd speedup is 1.47-1.50x depending on the order: the 1.48x in the REPORT §6.3 summary is the mean of the two orders, while the 1.50x in wt-llama31 README §7 takes only o1.

The e2e row is not a clean same-code, same-method comparison; the 1.44x is for reference only:
- The before value of 1,947 ms is the per-step median of the asm arm in 09-28 a0_p3a, from that single process only (the reverse-order a0_p3b produced NaN and was voided). REPORT §6.4 has voided the 09-28 A0 absolute numbers wholesale, and E2E-PLAN.md §1 also records them as "void numbers, keep only the lessons". This process called FlyDSL fwd for the first time at step 2, and the fwd tree's `_env.py:26` repointed `HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH` to the host library `~/.local/hipblaslt-gfx1250` at import; whether the later asm steps were affected was not verified (REPORT §5.3).
- The after value comes from a separately written suite of 10-02, `1002__e2e/e2e/`: fwd uses `fwd_r16_imglib`, a copy with those two repointing lines deleted, the adapter layer adds a BLAS guard, and steps 1-2 run ASM only (E2E-PLAN.md §2-§3).
- Between the two runs, the driver was first changed on 09-28/29 via -2410994 and -2411826 to 7.1.0-2411946 (the one in place for the 09-30 op re-measurement), and then on 10-01 again to 7.1.0-2412954, one change more than for the op rows.
- The only things the two sides have in common are: nkfix (`0927__b0/gemm/nkfix_b0.py`, unchanged in the git history since 09-28 05:12) plus `NKFIX_CHECK=1`, the same 32L config template (`0927__b0/e2e/configs/l8b_e2e.template.yaml`), and the practice of alternating 3 arms step by step over ~90 steps. wt-llama31 README §7 also counts this row as "Same code and method", which is inaccurate.

### a.2 Op-level problems

| # | Problem | Symptom / numbers | Cause | Impact | How it was resolved later | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Under the image's torch 2.11, main crashes as soon as it is imported | `import primus_turbo.pytorch` raises `TypeError: Opaque type ... must subclass torch._opaque_base.OpaqueBase`. All 4 candidate images have torch `2.11.0+rocm7.14.0a20260625`, and every one of them is hit. Test collection fails along with it | `core/low_precision.py` calls `register_opaque_type` at module level on `Float8QuantConfig`, `Float4QuantConfig` and `ScalingRecipe` (introduced by #483). torch 2.11 requires these classes to use the `OpaqueBaseMeta` metaclass. This problem is unrelated to attention | The whole package is unusable: not a single op, e2e run or test can start | On 09-13 the shim was hand-ported from the newer `/workspace/Primus-Turbo` in the image. When reproducing main on B0 on 09-14, this patch also had to be applied separately. Upstream fixed it in `f5e1f18b` (#501, 09-14). In addition, `build_ext` cannot run unless the `3rdparty/hipify_torch` submodule is initialized | 0913/phase1/RESULTS.md §5; 0914__repro__c07/RESULTS.md "Reproduction environment"; git show f5e1f18b |
| 2 | Default dispatch cannot pick the only backend that runs on gfx1250 | When no backend is pinned, dispatch for `flash_attn_func` (BSHD b4) goes as follows: FLYDSL is tried first and rejects G=4; then `_DENSE_FWD_BACKENDS` is scanned in insertion order (FLYDSL→AITER→HIPKITTENS→GLUON→TRITON), and AITER matches first. The `amdprimus` image has no aiter, so the very first training step fails with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'aiter'` → `ImportError: Primus-Turbo requires amd-aiter==0.1.14.post1`. If aiter is installed, fwd passes, but bwd fails midway through a training step with `invalid argument for fmha_bwd` | `DenseAttnFwdAiterBackend.can_handle` returns True for any 4-D fp16/bf16 input without looking at the architecture. The AITER path ultimately lands in CK, and CK's fmha only has CDNA kernels | The only backend that runs on gfx1250 is the Triton backend added by `c1325c7e`, whose `can_handle` accepts only `is_gfx1250`, but default dispatch never reaches it; the only option is to set `PRIMUS_TURBO_ATTN_BACKEND=TRITON` by hand. Moreover, the error surfaces midway through training, not at dispatch time | On the branch, `f8c45dee` (09-13) makes AITER dense refuse calls on gfx1250. The 09-30 port branch `dev/lhz/llama31_attn_opt` makes FLYDSL resolve to the new kernels on gfx1250 (G∈{1,2,4,8,16}, d128, bf16). origin/main `8cda13c7` has still not fixed it | 0913/phase0/PHASE0-STATUS.md:11-22; git show c1325c7e (commit message; `attention_impl.py:122-127, 431-436, 621-626`); session 541e7bc3 (aiter ImportError); 0930__port/PR_BODY.md |
| 3 | The FlyDSL arch gate `>= (9,5)` is open for gfx1250 (latent bug) | gfx1250 reports cc (12,5), so `_flydsl_common_ok` lets it through. Yet every gfx950 FlyDSL FA builder hard-raises on anything other than gfx950 | The gate was written as `get_device_compute_capability() >= _GFX950`; it should be an equality check | It did not blow up on 09-13 only because `_gqa_group_ok` happened to reject G=4. After upstream `9f05cd85` (#509, 09-17) relaxed the GQA gate to powers of 2 in [1,256], the two gates that had been covering for each other opened on main at the same time: Llama calls with SBHD or b=1 BSHD enter the gfx950 JIT and fail with `requires gfx950+ (uses ds_read_tr16_b64)`. BSHD b4 still falls through to AITER/CK, see #2 | On the branch, `f8c45dee` and `d00ba261` change it to `is_gfx950()`; on the port branch, `c36cc124` (09-30) changes it to an equality check. origin/main `8cda13c7` still has `>= _GFX950`. The 0916 addendum in 0914__campaign/HANDOFF.md says "fixed on main", but the "main" there actually refers to our branch | PHASE0-STATUS.md:24-32; git show origin/main:primus_turbo/pytorch/kernels/attention/attention_impl.py:49,79-106; git show 9f05cd85; wt-llama31 README §2 |
| 4 | flydsl is nominally an optional dependency, yet it is imported unconditionally at module level | In `c1325c7e`, `setup.py:529-535` does not install flydsl for gfx1250 builds. But `attention_flydsl_impl.py` imports flydsl at module level, and `attention_impl` and `flash_attn_interface` in turn import it at module level. So in an environment without flydsl, `from primus_turbo.pytorch.ops.attention import flash_attn_func` fails outright. `sparse_mla_impl.py` has the same problem, and it has no arch gate either. There are 10 more module-level imports of the same kind under MoE, GEMM and quantization | No try/except guard; the install policy and the import policy contradict each other | The only usable Triton path on gfx1250 goes down with it. It runs today only because the image happens to ship flydsl 0.2.4 preinstalled (the host does not have it) | On the branch, `f8c45dee` added a `FLYDSL_AVAILABLE` guard, and `d00ba261` (09-17) extended it to sparse_mla. Upstream `eeb9e8d7` (#508, 09-21) switched to installing `flydsl==0.2.4` on all archs. That only sidesteps the problem, it does not fix it: the module-level imports on origin/main are still unguarded | PHASE0-STATUS.md:34-43; git show d00ba261; git show origin/main:setup.py:531-535 |
| 5 | Mutually exclusive flydsl versions: aiter needs 0.3.2, Primus-Turbo needs 0.2.4 (found 09-17) | aiter pins `flydsl==0.3.2` because its gfx1250 FlyDSL fwd does not compile under 0.2.4: the `ast_rewriter` in 0.2.4 does not accept a list as the state variable of a stateful dynamic-if. But 0.3.2 removed `flydsl.expr.buffer_ops`, which Primus-Turbo's FlyDSL code imports unconditionally, so the result is `ImportError: cannot import name 'buffer_ops' from 'flydsl.expr'` | The same package is pinned to two incompatible `==` versions within one process | aiter's gfx1250 FlyDSL fwd and primus_turbo cannot be used in the same process. That is why the turbo baseline of the 09-28 B0 e2e had to run in a separate process | On 09-17, a side install via `pip install --target ~/.local/flydsl032`, then reordering `sys.path`; the op-evolve jobs import only torch and aiter. On 09-30 the port branch ported fwd r16 and bwd r29 back to 0.2.4. The LLVM bundled with 0.2.4 miscompiles the address of the async LDS→global O store, which faults as soon as it runs on the card, so `buffer_store` is used instead, at a cost of about -1.3% on fwd and about -1.8% on bwd | 0917__flydsl/VENDOR-REPORT-aiter-gfx1250.md §3; 0917__flydsl/STAGE1-FWD.md §3-4; 0927__b0/e2e/RECON.md §3.3; 0930__port/PR_BODY.md |
| 6 | gfx950's FlyDSL attention cannot be ported, and G=4 is rejected | The fwd's `flash_attn_fwd.py:71-74` raises "requires gfx950+ (uses ds_read_tr16_b64)" outright (in `c1325c7e`, line 71 is the arch check and lines 72-74 are the raise; HANDOFF cites :71, SURVEY cites :72-74). bwd has 6 kernels (odo, lse-transpose, dq-reduce, slot-reduce, a16-unpermute, dkdv), and every one of them asserts gfx950. Probing directly with the LLVM in the container, `llvm.amdgcn.mfma.f32.32x32x16.bf16`, `ds.read.tr16.b64` and `permlane32.swap` all hit Cannot select on gfx1250. `warp_size=64` is hard-coded at `flydsl/utils/attn_helper.py:382`. main's `_gqa_group_ok` requires G to be a power of 2 in [8,256] | These kernels were hand-scheduled for the fragment layout of CDNA4 wave64 MFMA. gfx1250 is wave32 + WMMA + 320 KB LDS + TDM and has no MFMA at all | For Llama-3.1-8B on gfx1250, the FlyDSL backend is unusable on two counts. On 09-14 the port was estimated at 18-36 engineer-days ("FlyDSL Gate A BLOCKED"). What was retracted on 0916 was only the conclusion that "the FlyDSL route is a dead end as a whole"; the point that "the existing gfx950 kernels cannot be ported" still stands | No port. | 3 | e2e choked by GEMM/BLAS | Once it ran through on A0-VR on 09-13: 245 tps, 133.7 s/step, 14.17 TFLOPS, GPU 100% busy the whole time. The entire attention path is 35.7 ms × 32 = 1.14 s, under 1% of the step. In the `amdprimus` image, hipBLASLt fails with `HIPBLAS_STATUS_INVALID_VALUE` as soon as it is called, so the only option was `TORCH_BLAS_PREFER_HIPBLASLT=0`, falling back to rocBLAS. rocBLAS on an idle card: bf16 8192³ 40.12 ms = 27.4 TF/s; qkv 32768×4096×6144 24.0, mlp up 23.9, mlp down 26.1 TF/s. On the same card, Triton GEMM measured 1002.7 TF/s (09-11 E1, treated at the time as the roof; overtaken on 09-15 by hipBLASLt NT at 1502 TF/s on the same card at the same clock, see the right-hand column) | The attribution at the time was "the image lacks the gfx1250 Tensile library". This attribution was later rewritten twice, see the right-hand column | No e2e number from before the optimization can either verify or falsify an attention change. JIRA's MI455X numbers (GEMM 655 ms/step, 19,795 tok/s) cannot have come from A0 with this image | The first rewrite came on 09-14: on B0 (container fa-e2e) the library was present and hipBLASLt worked, but at only 112.98 TF/s, while a naive Triton GEMM reached 1190.07 TF/s, a 10.5x gap. The second rewrite came on 09-15: the problem with the A0 image turned out to be a path mismatch: the 402 files sit in the `library/gfx1250/` subdirectory, but the loader looks one level up. With `HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH=…/_rocm_sdk_libraries_gfx1250/lib/hipblaslt/library/gfx1250` set, 8192³ NN reaches 68.74 TF/s, and 32L e2e goes 244→2,027 tps (8.3x, 3 steps, n=1, A0-VR). This fix has two environment traps: the library copy under `_rocm_sdk_devel/lib/hipblaslt/library/gfx1250` is incomplete and pointing at it gives a core dump / SIGSEGV, so only the `_rocm_sdk_libraries_gfx1250` copy can be used; a `bash -lc` login shell reads a profile script that forces `TORCH_BLAS_PREFER_HIPBLASLT=0` (our container has one), so use `bash -c`, set both variables explicitly, and then check `torch.backends.cuda.preferred_blas_library()` inside the process. The same day the real big-ticket item was found: hipBLASLt ships a pure-bf16 tuned library only for the forward's TN layout (`BB_BB_UA_Type_BB_HPA`); dgrad (NN, `Ailk_Bljk`) and wgrad go through a GridBased nearest-neighbor table lookup and land on the GEMV tile `MT32x16x32`, reaching only 50-80 TF/s, 16.5-21.5x slower than TN at the same shape; whereas, on the same card at the same clock, the torch-level NT path (`a @ bt.T` / `F.linear`, which lands on hipBLASLt's TN tuned library) measured 1502 TF/s at 8192³, faster even than the Triton 1002.7 treated as the roof on 09-11. The e2e cost while GEMM-NN was unfixed: on B0 on 09-28 (hipBLASLt path fixed, no nkfix), whichever attention was used, 32L reached only 1,856-1,922 tok/s (low-tier median; high tier 2,008-2,022), 17,049-17,655 ms/step, of which the backward `MT32x16x32` GEMMs took 15.3-16.1 s/step. Fixes: on B0 on 09-14, compile plus forced inductor Triton GEMM, 4.01x faster; on 09-15/16, nkfix (rewriting the operand layout of `aten::mm` with TorchDispatchMode); on 09-28, nkfix_b0 + transpose_triton ported to B0, with tps about 10 times the pre-fix level. | 4 | GPU memory right at the limit | With 32L, mbs4, s8192, AC none, the peak is 379.50 GiB (87.85%); flex is at 378.00 GiB. With AC full it drops to 174.79 GiB (40.5%), yet throughput is 215 tps, which shows memory is not the bottleneck. The default flavor `8B` goes through SDPA: CUDNN and FLASH are unavailable on gfx1250, and the fallback to MATH materializes a [4,32,8192,8192] matrix, pushing memory up to 442 GB and then SIGBUS, so the config was switched to `8B_flex`. Once during debugging, a previous training process had not exited yet and still held 379.5 GiB, which was misdiagnosed as a "leak" | 8B parameters + Adam + full activations by themselves need ~378 GiB | After that, any +0.3% change in memory could trigger SIGBUS: on 09-15, adding a persistent scratch cache raised the peak to 381.44 GiB (88.30%), and it hit SIGBUS | Switched to an 8L flavor that changes only the layer count for A/B; before launch, confirm that no leftover process remains on the GPU. See (d) | 0913/phase1/RESULTS.md §10-§11; Primus:…/repro_l8b_bf16_mbs4_seq8k_turbo.yaml:20-24 (comments); 0915__opt/E2E-AB.md:80-92 |
| 5 | Tensile split-K GEMM memory fault leaves a giant core dump | On the 5th e2e attempt on 09-13, a GPU memory fault hit `Cijk_Ailk_Bjlk_BBS_BH_MT64x1x64_…_GSU7` (Tensile split-K, with a shape like the LM head), writing a 311 GB GPU coredump. The same day, when op-level processes exited abnormally, they also wrote two 8 GB core dumps and one 7.8 GB core dump (owned by root, landing in the repo directory), and disk usage reached 80% at one point | Not attributed. The main training GEMMs all pass when tested individually; possibly the intermittent page faults already on record for this card | A single fault can fill up the disk | Retried with coredumps disabled, and the run went through; core dumps were added to gitignore. Later the rule became: no fp32 GEMMs on the card, only the image's bundled hipBLASLt, see (d) | session 541e7bc3 2026-09-13T05:25:48Z, 06:26:24Z-06:28:33Z, 08:17:58Z |

Notes:
- The e2e of "main + `PRIMUS_TURBO_ATTN_BACKEND=TRITON`" has never been measured. The 10-05 tutorial estimated it from op-level times and in-training attention times at ~1.9-2.05 s/step (~16-17k tok/s). This estimate presupposes that nkfix and the other GEMM fixes are already in place, and it is on A0-RF, so it is not the "pre-optimization" state (wt-llama31 README §2, §7).
- The 32L e2e baseline of 1,984 tok/s used by the later ladder comes from A0-VR on 09-15, when the hipBLASLt path had already been corrected and attention was already ASM fwd + fused bwd, so it is not a main number. See (b)(c).
- The 245 tok/s of A0 on 09-13 and the 2,394 tok/s of B0 on 09-14 cannot be treated as a comparison under the same config: the clocks differ (A0-VR vs B0), and so do the BLAS paths (A0 uses rocBLAS, B0 uses hipBLASLt). Both machines run torchtitan v0.2.2 (see a.1). Both are choked by BLAS, with attention taking under 1% (A0) or only a small share (B0).
- e2e baselines while the GEMM-NN problem was unfixed (hipBLASLt path fixed, no nkfix): 09-15 A0-VR 32L 2,027 tok/s (3 steps, n=1); 09-28 B0 32L 1,856-1,922 tok/s (17.0-17.7 s/step, attention already one of the three: Triton / ASM / FlyDSL), see a.3 #3.

**Summary.** Before optimization, running Llama-3.1-8B attention with main on gfx1250 had three layers of problems:
1. By default it does not run at all. Under torch 2.11 it crashes on import; the default dispatch lands on CK, which supports only CDNA; all tests are skipped, so there is no signal whatsoever.
2. The only backend that can be pinned by hand, Triton, uses a single config copied verbatim from CDNA and is ~1.9x slower than the incumbent flex, with dkdv accounting for 61.5%.
3. e2e cannot detect any attention change. First, the image's BLAS was broken or extremely slow (rocBLAS 27.4 TF/s; later traced to a path mismatch, plus the missing tuned library for the NN layout). Second, `converters: []` meant that what ran in e2e was always flex. Third, Primus's turbo default config switches on FP8/MX linear as well; in the 09-14 "turbo vs flex 1.459x", both arms were flex, and the real source of the difference is still unresolved.

In addition, all numbers on A0 were taken under the VR throttle (1100 MHz), and from 09-04..09-13 this card wedged on average about once every two days (5 times in total). So every absolute number in this section must carry a machine and clock label, and for cross-machine and cross-clock ratios it must also be made clear which kind they are (see "Notation and conventions").

## (b) Performance of each backend after adaptation

**Op shape**: Llama-3.1-8B attention, b4 s8192 hq32 hkv8 d128 bf16, causal (Sq=Skv; bottom-right and top-left are equivalent), BSHD, GQA 4.

**FLOP convention**: all TF/s in the tables are computed with op-evolve `tools/op_flops.py`: fwd 2.199292e12, bwd 5.498229e12 (bwd uses the nominal 5-GEMM count). The source documents of 09-13..09-15 report only the fwd+bwd total (7.697e12 / total time); per-direction TF/s marked `*` in the tables are converted from per-direction times. The in-tree vendored fused backward actually issues 7 GEMM passes, issued = 7.6975e12 = 1.400× nominal (7/5); our FlyDSL bwd has the same 7-GEMM structure plus diagonal-block granularity (×1.00546), issued = 7.7395e12 = 1.40764× nominal. This section never uses the issued convention (`0914__repro__c07/RESULTS.md:121-122`; `0923__flydsl/hint.md:2104-2110`).

**vs ASM** = this row's time / the aiter ASM time in the same epoch; >1 means slower than ASM.
- "same-process": measured alternating with ASM in the same process; the most trustworthy.
- "≈": same machine and same clock state, but cross-session or cross-ruler; only the order of magnitude is meaningful.
- Absolute values are not comparable across epochs; only ratios within the same epoch can be compared.

**Source paths**: relative to `Primus-Turbo/output/` by default.
- `OE:` = `/home/lihuzhan/code/2026_0910__op-evolve/op-evolve`
- `skill:` = `Primus-Turbo/.claude/skills/gfx1250-attn-campaign/`
- `WT:` = worktree `wt-llama31` (branch `dev/lhz/llama31_attn_opt`)

**Measurement epochs** (the tables below are grouped by these):

| Code | Machine / clock state | Date | Main ruler |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | A0 `heliosr-1b114-c07-1` single card, VR throttle (DPM cap 1100 MHz, ~943–1069 MHz under load) | 09-10..09-17 | `tools/gfx1250/tune_attention.py`: iters 20 / warmup 5, median of CUDA events, 256 MiB L2 flush per rep, SQNR ≥ 50 dB on four tensors; timing includes the autograd plumbing |
| E2 | B0 `ctheliosp-1b112-a37-1`, 4 cards loaded simultaneously, 2133–2244 MHz | 09-14 | same tune_attention.py as E1 |
| E3 | A0 still throttled (prod timing window 989–1069 MHz) | 09-17..09-27 | op-evolve `benchmark.py`: times kernel launches only, median n=51, palindromic interleaving, 3 s warmup; `fwdab.py` ABBA |
| E3′ | A0's last measurement before the firmware reflash, still VR-throttled (op-level sclk 1020–1061 MHz); VBIOS 630A, SMU 125.7.1, driver amdgpu-dkms 7.1.1-2397345 | 09-28 | blocked randn ruler (prod: one process per order; A/A replicas in the same process); e2e: 3 arms alternating per step in the same process |
| E4 | B0 (2356 MHz idle; clocks not recorded per run at op level; 1.25–1.45 GHz during training) | 09-27..09-28 | per-call interleaving at first; from 09-28 switched to blocked timing (fwd ~03:40, bwd ~05:00 UTC, lead 4 + block 9, A/A ±0.2%); also `opcheck.py` |
| E5 | A0 after the 09-29 firmware reflash: VBIOS 700E, SMU 125.12.0, op level 1.74–2.03 GHz, ~1.5 GHz in training; driver 7.1.0-2411946 on 09-29, 7.1.0-2412954 from 10-01 | 09-30..10-05 | blocked randn, same process; on 10-02 also two real-data rulers, blk / gb; `bench_attention_turbo.py` |

A0 absolute numbers from E1/E3/E3′ are void after the 09-29 firmware reflash. After the reflash the clock rose by ~1.75×, but the speedup is not uniform across kernels (ASM fwd 1.24×, FlyDSL bwd 1.67×), so old data cannot be converted with a single factor (`0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md` §6.3). With the same blocked ruler and the same set of arms, r13ns/ASM fell from 1.29 in E3′ to 1.08 in E5 and r29/ASM from 1.44 to 1.20: the ratios themselves also shift with the clock epoch. The firmware reflash and the driver change fell in the same period (7.1.1-2397345 → 7.1.0-2411946, then -2412954 on 10-01), so the effects cannot be strictly attributed to one or the other (ibid. §6.3). The same holds for e2e: the ASM arm going from 1,947 ms in E3′ to 1,350.6 ms in E5 (≈1.44×) is not purely a platform difference — besides the firmware, the driver changed twice between the two runs; moreover, in that E3′ process (a0_p3a), after the first FlyDSL call (step 2), `HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH` had already been repointed to the host library by the fwd tree's `_env.py`, and whether hipBLASLt rereads this variable mid-run has not been verified (`0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md` §5.3).

### b.1 What adaptations it took to get these backends running

| Backend | Key adaptations | commit / path |
|---|---|---|
| Primus-Turbo Triton dense backend (main #481 `c1325c7e`, the only in-tree backend that runs on gfx1250) | <ul><li>Under torch 2.11, `register_opaque_type` in `low_precision.py` made the import fail; patched locally first</li><li>Default dispatch could not pick Triton: AITER registers first and its `can_handle` accepts any architecture, so dispatch landed on the CDNA-only CK and the backward failed with invalid argument. The fix keeps the AITER dense backend out of dispatch on gfx1250</li><li>The FlyDSL gate was written as `>=(9,5)`, which gfx1250 also passes; changed to an exact gfx950 check; added a guard around flydsl's module-level import</li><li>conftest skipped everything on gfx1250; changed to an opt-out marker; runnable tests 0 → 36 → 51</li><li>The autotune list had only one config, copied from CDNA; made it tunable (`PRIMUS_TURBO_ATTN_TRITON_TUNE`): fwd `num_stages=2`, bwd `num_warps=2`</li></ul> | <ul><li>upstream `f5e1f18b` (#501)</li><li>`f8c45dee`, `df20f1d6`, `5eba2cf4`, `d00ba261`</li><li>product branch `c36cc124`</li></ul> |
| In-tree vendored aiter onekernel fused Triton backward | <ul><li>Vendored aiter-src @`ffa945f9` (MIT, ~1790 lines); gated on parallelism rather than sequence length</li><li>Fixed it being unusable under torch.compile</li><li>fwd `num_warps=2` plus no `waves_per_eu` in bwd: total +4.28%</li><li>`TRITON_HIP_USE_IN_THREAD_TRANSPOSE`: backward −9%</li></ul> | `1cb2e183`, `03a76f61`→`8dd2fd32`, `ad67a2cc`, `566e7798`, `a009599b` |
| aiter Triton MHA (measurement only, not in-tree) | <ul><li>The amdprimus image has no aiter; git-cloned aiter-src separately (09-13 @`ffa945f9`, from 09-17 @`6963ae9d`)</li><li>aiter's CK/HIP JIT fails to compile at import on gfx1250, but the Triton ops are still usable</li></ul> | `tune_attention.py --impl aiter`; `OE: output/0911__fa_gfx1250_phase1/E2.md` §3 |
| torch flex | <ul><li>Ships with torchtitan; no changes needed</li><li>Autotune is mandatory on B0 (num_warps=4). The fwd+bwd total of 37.122 ms (fwd 6.894 / bwd 30.228, B0 GPU0 2356 MHz) measured with the default heuristic (bwd num_warps=8) is retracted; after autotune the total is 15.26–15.31 ms</li></ul> | `0914__repro__c07/flex_ledger.jsonl:1-3`; `0914__repro__c07/RESULTS.md:89-98` |
| aiter ASM fwd (`fmha_bf16_pertokenBf16_hd128_128x256_mask.co`) | <ul><li>Swapped in by the dispatch layer when aiter is importable; ASM fwd must be enabled together with the fused bwd (conjunctive gate)</li><li>Added a hard-off switch `PRIMUS_TURBO_ATTN_DISABLE_ASM_FWD`</li><li>Wrote a launcher that calls the `.co` directly</li><li>Installed psutil in the container; the first call JIT-compiles C++ and needs hipcc</li></ul> | `0e5cb743`, `c93057b6`, `20f2ccde`, `e4efebe2` (all on `dev/lhz/attn`, not upstreamed) |
| aiter ASM bwd (odo → `bwd_hd128_bf16_causal_br_a32_pssk` → dq_convert) | <ul><li>aiter's Python entry point does not select gfx1250; wrote our own ~674-line launcher, with the kernarg field table generated from the ELF and self-checked</li><li>`dkdv_heads='q'` plus a host reduction to avoid the GQA out-of-bounds write</li><li>Eligibility gate seqlen ≥ 2048; non-causal allowed</li><li>scratch reuse; HipModule made a process-wide singleton</li><li>Off by default (enabled only with `PRIMUS_TURBO_ATTN_ENABLE_ASM_BWD=1`)</li><li>op-evolve's beat arm loads `_asm_bwd_kernargs.py` by file path and does not import primus_turbo</li></ul> | <ul><li>`c871cd47`, `adc367f0`, `c765edd4`, `46b1774a`, `f8155ae9`, `b281b46b`, `514e4a21`, `cb3ed519`</li><li>`tools/gfx1250/asm_bwd_{abi,launcher}.py`</li><li>`OE: …/job_context/op/beat/impl.py`</li></ul> |
| aiter FlyDSL fwd m32x8 | <ul><li>Requires flydsl 0.3.2: the ast_rewriter of 0.2.4 does not accept a list as a state variable</li><li>0.3.2 is side-installed to `~/.local/flydsl032`. 0.3.2 removed `flydsl.expr.buffer_ops`, so it cannot share a process with turbo's 0.2.4 tree</li><li>Switched to 0.3.4.1 from 09-25; performance unchanged</li></ul> | `234ec33a` (`--impl flydsl`), `963e7d7d`; `0917__flydsl/STAGE1-FWD.md` §4 |
| Our FlyDSL fwd / bwd | <ul><li>op-evolve jobs: bwd pinned to 0.3.2, fwd uses 0.3.4.1</li><li>Product branch `dev/lhz/llama31_attn_opt` (based on origin/main `8cda13c7`) ported to the repo-pinned flydsl 0.2.4</li><li>The LLVM in 0.2.4 miscompiles the address of the async LDS→global O store; switched to `buffer_store`, at a cost of fwd −1.3%, bwd −1.8%</li></ul> | `5658a443`, `e05b67fd`, `5a8446b5`, `c36cc124`; `primus_turbo/flydsl/attention/gfx1250/` |
| e2e integration (shared by all backends) | <ul><li>Accept `enable_gqa`. Previously the training config used `converters: []` to dodge a TypeError, so turbo attention never entered e2e</li><li>`pip uninstall primus_turbo` inside the container: the editable 0.4.1.dev12 in the image shadowed the checkout</li><li>`HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH` points at the image library (the `library/gfx1250` subdirectory)</li><li>nkfix works around bad hipBLASLt NN/wgrad tiles; rewritten on B0 as `nkfix_b0.py` + `transpose_triton.py`</li><li>e2e backend-switch shim (`E2E_ATTN`, no changes to Primus / Turbo source)</li></ul> | `1babf4ce`, `4fec7712`, `23fdc0bc`, `f08ccffe`; `0927__b0/gemm/`, `0927__b0/e2e/`, `1002__e2e/`; `WT: docs/gfx1250_llama31_8b_e2e/` |

### Overview: latest comparable readings per backend

For each backend only its most recent epoch with an ASM comparison is listed (epoch definitions in the E1–E5 table above). Absolute values in different rows come from different epochs and cannot be compared across rows; look only at each row's own "vs ASM". TF/s uses the op_flops nominal convention, and values marked `*` are converted from times; o1 / o2 are the two arm orders of the blocked ruler; e2e is 32 layers by default, and ms values marked † are converted from tps; ratios labeled "cross-tool", "cross-ruler" or "cross-process" indicate the order of magnitude only. Cells with no reading at all say "not measured". Main gaps:
- E5 (after the reflash) measured only ASM, three FlyDSL variants (aiter m32x8, our champion, the product port) and main Triton; flex, aiter Triton and the vendored fused Triton have no E5 numbers.
- After nkfix, flex and aiter Triton have no e2e numbers; turbo Triton has only an estimate on A0, and its only actual measurement is a single one on B0 in E4.

**Overall speedup of the product branch over the main Triton dense backend** (E5, A0, flydsl 0.2.4, `bench_attention_turbo.py`, 09-30): geometric mean over the 36 shapes admitted by the FlyDSL gate (causal 18 + non-causal 18) gives causal fwd **4.13×** / bwd **3.37×**, non-causal fwd **3.47×** / bwd **3.58×** (`0930__port/PR_BODY.md:36-41`; recomputing from the two CSVs gives the same result).

**Caveat when citing these two bench CSVs**: the bench's built-in reference is itself faulty on non-causal s8192; every backend reports dq ≈12 dB and is marked FAIL (TRITON 15 rows; FLYDSL 9 rows, and FLYDSL has another 54 rows that are ERROR because the gate does not admit them), whereas a CPU fp32 reference gives 52 dB. These FAILs are a problem with the reference, not kernel errors (`0930__port/runs/bench_turbo_triton.log:48`, `bench_turbo_flydsl.log:72`; session fdc2534d .txt:474).

#### b.2.3 Backends that did not run or were eliminated

| Backend | Result | Reason | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| aiter CK fmha (main's default dispatch lands here) | Failed | <ul><li>CK supports CDNA only: fwd runs, bwd fails mid-training with `invalid argument for fmha_bwd`</li><li>aiter's CK/HIP JIT fails to compile at import on gfx1250 (`ck_tile/core/config.hpp:576` "Only one target architecture can be defined", 9 errors in total), then prints "CK and HIP ops are disabled"</li></ul> | `git show c1325c7e`; `OE: output/0911__fa_gfx1250_phase1/E2.md` §3 |
| HipKittens (main #462 supports gfx950 only; there is also a udna1 port to gfx1250) | Failed, judged RED | Stuck at three compile-time hard walls: <ul><li>`reductions.cuh` uses the gfx950-only `permlane32_swap` (7 sites) and does not compile</li><li>`mma_AB` / `mma_AtB` still dispatch to wave64 MFMA; only `mma_ABt` compiles</li><li>`swap_layout` covers only half of the registers under wave32</li></ul>The GPU gate gets 126 of 256 elements wrong (worst abs 2.73). 53 of the 72 header files are byte-identical to cdna4 | `0914__campaign/RESULTS.md` §8; `0914__hk_udna1_gate/` |
| Gluon (main #469) | Not applicable | Supports gfx950 only, and forward only (the gate of `DenseAttnFwdGluonBackend` accepts only gfx950); never run on gfx1250 | `git show e18cf0a1`; session fdc2534d |
| Primus-Turbo's built-in FlyDSL attention (gfx950) | Failed | Depends on MFMA 32x32x16, `ds_read_tr16_b64` and `permlane32_swap`, all three of which report "Cannot select" on gfx1250; `warp_size=64` is hard-coded. In addition, the original `>=(9,5)` gate would dispatch it to gfx1250; it only avoided blowing up because G=4 happens to be rejected by `_gqa_group_ok` | `0914__campaign/RESULTS.md` §9; `f8c45dee` |
| torch SDPA FLASH (AOTriton) | Runs, but slowest | fwd+bwd total 97.063 ms on throttled A0 (~79 TF/s, total convention). The commit message of `c1325c7e` claims 161.4 TF/s measured on the B2 shape, with Triton at 220.6 TF/s (measurement conditions unknown, see a.2 #11) | `0913__opt_plan__claude/phase1/RESULTS.md` §1; `git show c1325c7e` |
| aiter Triton `mha_fused_bwd` | Numerically wrong, eliminated | Total 49.051 ms, but dk is only −0.22 dB. Also, onekernel bwd with only `BLOCK_N1=256` changed gives dq of only 9.59 dB, a silent error | `OE: output/0911__fa_gfx1250_phase1/E2.md` §4-6 |
| aiter ASM `*_pssk_perf.co` | Unusable | dk/dv are bitwise identical to the shipped version, but dq is only 5.84 dB; it is also not in the dispatch CSV | `0915__opt/PERF-CO-CLOSED.md` |

**Key points**:
- **Triton family**: the best Triton bwd (aiter tuned version, vendored fused version plus ITT) is still about 2–2.4 times ASM on throttled A0. Of these, ≈2.30–2.40× is a cross-ruler ratio: the E1 tune ruler (including ~1.4 ms of autograd) against the E3 benchmark.py bar of 7.68 ms; 2.01× is a same-process ratio, but its denominator is the bring-up ASM reading of 8.661 ms, which is on the slow side. The best fwd is about 1.5 times (E2 aiter Triton tuned upper bound ≈1.49×, same machine, cross-session). The gap is structural: a single ASM workgroup uses the full 320 KiB of LDS and 1024 VGPRs, and the bwd has 864 `v_wmma` instructions, while Triton allocates only 64 KB of LDS. Tuning Triton's parameters cannot close this gap (`0913__opt_plan__claude/phase2/T2-ASM-BACKWARD-SPEC.md`).
- **fwd sensitivity to the clock**: in the throttled-A0 era the fwd gap was amplified by the clock. The aiter FlyDSL fwd to ASM ratio was 1.51–1.53× on throttled A0 and dropped to 1.125× after the reflash. The reason is that ASM fwd barely changes with sclk, whereas FlyDSL scales with sclk.
- **An op-level lead does not necessarily carry over to training**: at the training operating point (after a GEMM burst, ~1.28 GHz), s6 goes from 2.8% ahead to 3.8% behind, and fwd r16 goes from 8% behind to 35% behind.

### b.3 e2e performance (Llama-3.1-8B)

**Common configuration** (unless otherwise noted): BF16, single card, MBS=GBS=4, seq 8192 (32,768 tokens per step), AC none, torch.compile off, 32 layers. "8L" is an 8-layer trimmed version, used only for the A0 09-15/16 GEMM ladder.

**Conventions**:
- tokens/s is per-card throughput.
- TFLOP/s is torchtitan's self-reported value; values marked † are converted from tps using the torchtitan convention (32L ~57.9 GFLOP/token, 8L ~16.84).
- torchtitan's MFU uses the A100's 312 TF/s as the denominator, so it is entirely unusable on this card.

| **E1 · A0 VR throttle** | | | | | | | |

**e2e key points**:
- **Before nkfix**, e2e was choked by GEMM; attention was only a small slice of each step, and backend differences were essentially invisible:
  - A0 32L rocBLAS: under 1%. The 09-13 run (nominally turbo Triton, most likely actually flex), computed as 35.7 ms/layer × 32 = 1.14 s, is ~0.85% (`0913__opt_plan__claude/phase1/RESULTS.md:266`); the 09-15 ASM path, computed as 11.7 ms/layer × 32 ≈ 374 ms, is 0.28% (`0915__opt/RESULTS.md:169`), but the ASM bwd inside that 11.7 ms used the later-retracted shim reading of 10.160 ms, so the actual share is lower;
  - A0 8L hipBLASLt image library: 3.3% (measured with a profile, `0915__opt/BOTTLENECK-SHIFT.md:14`);
  - B0 32L: 1.4–5.4% (`0927__b0/e2e/RESULT.md:37`);
  - A0 09-13: turbo and flex differ by 0.4%; B0 09-28: FlyDSL vs ASM is 1.0045.
- **After nkfix**, attention's share of each step:
  - turbo Triton 43.9% (B0);
  - ASM 17.0–17.4% (B0), 14.9% (A0);
  - FlyDSL 20.5–21.8% (B0), 15.3% (A0, s6).
- **Evolution of the FlyDSL-to-ASM per-step ratio**: on B0, r6+r20 is 1.0475–1.0500, r13ns+r20 is 1.0403–1.0408, r16+r29 is 1.0323–1.0328; on A0 10-02, r16+r29 is 1.027 and r16+s6 is **0.999**. Within the r16+s6 combination:
  - bwd is ~4 ms per step faster than ASM: the median of adjacent-pair differences is 4.50 / 4.56 ms (two processes), i.e. the 4.5 in the body text of `RESULT-e2e.md`; subtracting the two arms' own medians gives 3.8–4.4 ms (166.5 vs 162.2 / 162.6 in the `RESULT-e2e.md` table, i.e. 3.9–4.3).
  - fwd is 9.2 ms per step slower (adjacent pairs 9.18 / 9.24). Looking at attention alone, the remaining gap is in fwd.
  - What does not add up: summed over attention (fwd+bwd), fly takes ~4.3–5.2 ms more per step, yet the step is 0.7–2.0 ms faster. In other words, everything outside attention takes ~6 ms less per step on the fly arm (step − FA in `e2e/runs/analysis.1002_095504.txt` is −6.3 ms in both processes; flyr29 vs asm is −21 / −22 ms). Where outside attention this difference arises, and how much of it is noise, has not been broken down.
- The product branch `dev/lhz/llama31_attn_opt` still ships bwd r29 (1.235× ASM at op level under flydsl 0.2.4); s6 has not been ported into it yet.
- **The tutorial's e2e number cannot be compared directly with ASM**: the tutorial's 1,361.9 ms/step is `NKFIX_CHECK=0`, while ASM's 1,350.6 ms is the 10-02 `NKFIX_CHECK=1`. The two differ by only 0.8%, but this does not mean the branch is only 0.8% slower than ASM. Adjusting for the ~33 ms/step that CHECK=1 adds (a value measured from a B0 trace and reused by the tutorial; `0927__b0/gemm/REPORT.md` §5), ASM under CHECK=0 is ~1,318 ms and the branch is ~1.03× of it, i.e. ~3% slower (estimated, not measured). This is consistent with the 1.027 vs ASM of the 10-02 r16+r29 (flydsl 0.3.4.1), which is also CHECK=1.
- **The PR body cites an old number from another machine**: `0930__port/PR_BODY.md:43` says "step time is 1.032x that of the ASM kernels" without naming the machine. This is the B0 09-28 r16+r29 reading (1.0323 / 1.0328, flydsl 0.3.4.1 tree); on A0 10-02 the same combination is 1.027, and r16+s6 is 0.999 (`1002__e2e/RESULT-e2e.md`). The PR body needs to be updated.
- **Not comparable across machines**: the e2e absolute values of B0 (ASM 1,579 ms) and post-reflash A0 (ASM 1,351 ms) cannot be compared directly; the platform and the training clock both differ (the B0 final r16+r29 runs are at 1,342 / 1,371 MHz, the n5/n6 runs at 1,383 / 1,394 MHz; A0 10-02 median ~1.50 GHz).
- **NaN correction**: on A0 09-16, 21% of the nkfix runs hit NaN, so several numbers were corrected or voided: 38,043 → 37,746, +14.40% → +12.72%, 49,878 and 11,608 void. The B0 rewrite ran 8 times over 486 steps with 0 NaN.

### b.4 Problems with aiter ASM, the best-performing backend

| Problem | Symptom / impact | Our handling | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| bwd has no callable gfx1250 Python entry point | <ul><li>`can_impl_fmha_v3_bwd` only recognizes gfx942/gfx950; although the C++ host has 5 gfx1250 special cases, a call through aiter's normal entry point silently gets no ASM bwd</li><li>kernarg: `dqdkdv` is 704 B, 44 fields, each field 16 B aligned; `odo` is tightly packed and uses kernarg preloading, which cannot be confirmed by disassembly; the `dq_convert` header says 192 B, the ELF has 208 B; all strides are in bytes</li></ul> | <ul><li>Read the calling contract out of the ELF offline and wrote our own launcher of about 674 lines</li><li>The ABI self-check caught that the hand-copied field table had missed the last 7 fields (including `mask_x/mask_y`). For these two fields aiter itself passes uninitialized stack values on the mt=2 path; we pass 0, which is safe</li><li>`odo` is verified after pre-filling it with NaN</li></ul> | `0922_summary/ASM-ATTENTION.md` §1.1; `0913__opt_plan__claude/phase2/T2-ASM-BACKWARD-SPEC.md`; `skill: references/baselines.md` §3.2 |
| GQA out-of-bounds write (the most substantive correctness bug) | The pssk grid is `(kv_tiles, nhead_q, batch)`, yet it indexes the kv-sized dk/dv by q head, so with G=4, 4 WGs contend for the same tile without synchronization:<ul><li>s=1024: dk/dv silently corrupted, SQNR about −0.3 dB, dq still 52.24 dB</li><li>s=256: the out-of-bounds access crosses a page and the process faults (nothing in dmesg, the card is not harmed)</li><li>Localization experiment (ratio=4, dk/dv indexed by kv head): dk −0.94 / dv −0.65 dB; all correct at ratio=1</li><li>s=8192 with `dkdv_heads=kv`: triggers `GCVM_L2_PROTECTION_FAULT`, queues are reset per process, no AC-cycle spent</li></ul> | `dkdv_heads='q'` plus host reduction:<ul><li>dk/dv device memory 0.125 → 0.500 GiB, 1.254 GiB more at peak</li><li>Time: fp32-sum version +0.482 ms (5.2% of bwd); bf16-reduce version 0.2225 ms (2.88% of the bar)</li><li>dk/dv precision drops by 1.7–1.9 dB (50.55 / 51.09)</li></ul>Vendor report filed | `0917__flydsl/VENDOR-REPORT-aiter-gfx1250.md:15-38`; `0922_summary/ASM-ATTENTION.md:52-67`; `GQA-WORKAROUND-COST.md:50-65`; `skill: references/baselines.md` §3.3 |
| Not drop-in: 3 kernels, and the fp32 dq_acc must be zeroed on every call | <ul><li>odo → pssk (514 `buffer_atomic_add_f32` writing the fp32 dq_acc) → dq_convert (ts_dq=64)</li><li>Without zeroing you silently get garbage dq</li><li>6 dispatches per call</li><li>Per layer, dq_acc is 536 MB and dk/dv are 268 MB each</li><li>On B0 e2e the adaptation overhead is 9.2 ms/step (GQA summation 3.84, dq_convert 2.27, zeroing 1.62, odo 1.44), plus 1 GiB of resident scratch</li></ul> | `asm_backward()` allocates and zeroes internally; scratch is cached per shape (1.000 GiB process-wide). FlyDSL bwd directly outputs bf16 for the hkv heads, with none of this overhead | `T2-ASM-BACKWARD-SPEC.md`; `0927__b0/e2e/RESULT.md` §0; `e2e/BUILD.md` §0 |
| Precompiled .co only: thin assets, not tunable, not modifiable | <ul><li>gfx1250 has 52 .co files in total (gfx950 has 1466); only 6 for bwd (gfx950 has 124)</li><li>bwd has only a32 + pssk, bf16, hd128, batch mode. The `a16_psskddv` used on MI355 does not exist on gfx1250; no varlen bwd (dispatch has only mode=0, seqstart is passed as nullptr); no fp16 / swa / group</li><li>fwd has hd64, hd128 and varlen, but hd128 has no sink variant</li><li>VGPR 1024, LDS 327,680 B and wg 1024 are all fully used</li></ul> | Only call and wrap it, never modify the assembly. Suggested asking aiter for psskddv / a16 variants (no reply seen). Missing varlen and unmaintainability are the direct reasons the FlyDSL project was started | `0922_summary/ASM-ATTENTION.md` §4; `VENDOR-REPORT-aiter-gfx1250.md` §2; `0915__opt/BACKEND-STRATEGY.md:24-48` |
| Measurement artifact: 10.160 ms (retracted) | <ul><li>On 09-15 the `_AsmFwdAsmBwd` autograd shim in `tune_attention.py` called `asm_backward` without passing `hip=` / `scratch=`, so every call did 3× hipModuleLoad and a fresh allocation of ~1 GiB; it also included ~1.4 ms of autograd</li><li>The value was mislabeled as the "product path" and stayed in use until 09-24</li><li>The 541 TF/s, 11.726 ms/layer, 4.757×, 1.74× and "FlyDSL already at 0.92× bar" derived from it are all too high</li></ul> | Use the same-session measurement of the op-evolve beat arm as the reference: 7.6134 ms on 09-17, 7.6766 ms (n=224) / ~716 TF/s in the 09-24 census. FlyDSL's real progress at the time was 0.70–0.72× | `skill: references/baselines.md` §0, §5; `0924__flydsl/DAY-SUMMARY.md`; `0915__opt/E2E-AB.md:284-302` |
| Product integration: reloaded on every backward, scratch reallocated again and again, tight device memory | <ul><li>The product path `asm_dense_backward` did not pass a `HipModule`, so 32L did 96 hipModuleLoad calls per step and never unloaded them; in addition, every call allocated its own dq_acc (fp32) and per-q-head dk/dv. The op-level harness sees neither of these</li><li>~~A fresh allocation of about 1 GiB per layer makes 32L e2e 6.78% slower (n=1)~~ **Retracted**: the scratch key does not include the layer index, so once cached it is 1.000 GiB process-wide and does not grow with the layer count; at 32L without caching, reserved memory is bitwise identical in both arms (380.06 GiB); the 6.78% (1,858 vs 1,984 tps) is n=1 and falls within the mode spacing of the multimodal e2e noise measured later (1.8% / 6.8% between clusters, cross-run sd ~3.9%)</li><li>After adding the scratch cache, 32L (88% device memory) wedged with SIGBUS at step 3 (+1.38 GiB, right up against the 88.30% ceiling), costing 1 AC-cycle</li></ul> | HipModule changed to a process-wide singleton (`514e4a21`); scratch reused (`b281b46b`). After both fixes, 8L, seed 1234, n=9 per arm: ON vs OFF is −0.28% (0.15× sem), no measurable e2e difference; the eligibility gate therefore stays off by default | `0915__opt/E2E-AB.md:94-133,180-282`; `ASM-ATTENTION.md` §1.4 |
| ~0.85 ms fixed overhead, so short sequences are actually slower | ASM bwd takes 0.88 / 0.91 / 1.00 / 1.34 ms over s1024→8192 (16× the work); s1024 loses at every degree of parallelism (0.537–0.660×) | The eligibility gate is set to seqlen ≥ 2048 (`f8155ae9`). But part of this overhead is precisely the per-call hipModuleLoad; after the fix the 9 support points need to be re-measured, which was never done | `0915__opt/RESULTS.md:99-124`; `E2E-AB.md:296-302` |
| dq uses fp32 atomic accumulation, non-deterministic | <ul><li>514 `buffer_atomic_add_f32 SCOPE_DEV`; dq run-to-run SQNR is 113.0 dB on fast and 98.0 dB on prod, not bitwise identical</li><li>When `is_deterministic` is requested, aiter returns −1 and falls back to CK</li><li>This conflicts with our "bitwise identical over 200 runs" contract and forces FlyDSL onto 7-GEMM, a structural factor of 1.386–1.408×</li></ul> | On 09-25 the determinism gate was split (`c741fa8d`): dk/dv must still be bitwise identical, dq changed to run-to-run SQNR ≥ 70 dB. FlyDSL s6's dq is bitwise identical run-to-run | `0925__flydsl/AITER-5GEMM-STUDY.md` §2; `0923__flydsl/hint.md` h18 / h41; `0930__bwd/REPORT.md` |
| fwd's LSE layout is incompatible with the in-tree two-kernel bwd | ASM fwd outputs a natural-log LSE of shape [B,Hq,Sq] and writes it even when `return_lse=False`; the in-tree two-kernel bwd reads a packed [B,Hq,2*Sq]. Mixing them raises no error, and the gradients go "smoothly wrong" | A conjunctive gate: ASM fwd is enabled only together with the fused bwd, and the decision is stored in ctx (`0e5cb743`) | `0913__opt_plan__claude/phase2/T7-ASM-FORWARD.md` (b); `0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:124-126` |
| Fragile dependency chain at the fwd entry point | <ul><li>`fmha_fwd_with_sink_asm` JIT-compiles C++ on first call (needs hipcc); architecture detection relies on rocminfo</li><li>Whether it can be enabled depends on the unrelated psutil: when the e2e container lacks psutil it silently falls back to Triton fwd, an op-level loss of 9.8%, and 51/51 tests still pass</li><li>`AITER_LOG_LEVEL=ERROR` swallows the banner, so in the B0 09-14 training ASM fwd was in fact never called</li><li>On 09-13 it hit `import jax` on A0, which was absent on B0 on 09-14; cause not verified</li></ul> | Lazy one-time import, permanently disabled on failure; psutil added to the container; the gate itself writes a trace file to prove it was called; added a hard-off switch `20f2ccde` for A/B | `0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:116-130,267-293`; `T7-ASM-FORWARD.md` (a) |
| Version conflict, cannot serve as an upstream product backend | <ul><li>aiter pins flydsl 0.3.2 and Primus-Turbo pins 0.2.4; the two cannot live in the same process</li><li>aiter's gfx1250 dispatch never reaches ASM; it can only be launched by hand via ctypes</li><li>The test image has no aiter: on the product branch the 28 tests that fall back to aiter fail</li></ul> | Side-installed `~/.local/flydsl032` and `flydsl0341`; the beat arm loads by file path. The product branch `dev/lhz/llama31_attn_opt` now ships FlyDSL instead, keeping ASM only as a control arm | `0917__flydsl/STAGE1-FWD.md` §4; `0930__port/PR_BODY.md` Testing; session fdc2534d 2026-10-05 |
| causal / layout / shape restrictions | <ul><li>bwd has only two kinds of .co: causal bottom-right (`causal_br`, CSV mask=2) and non-causal</li><li>The gate only admits self-attention: with Sq=Skv the two mask semantics are equivalent; cross-attention is rejected</li><li>Requires BSHD, a contiguous last dimension and hq%hkv==0; no support for sink, sliding window, bias, alibi, dropout or fp16 (the fwd gate has 12 rejection conditions in total)</li><li>aiter has only tested gqa=8 on hd128</li></ul> | Gate by capability. gqa=4 verified on B0: SQNR for all 32 q heads is within 53.54–53.72 dB. Non-causal admitted, a 1.71× speedup | `0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:118-128,273-275`; `0915__opt/PROGRESS-REPORT.html` Q3 |
| Small dk/dv precision margin | In the host reduction each partial is first rounded to bf16, giving dk/dv 50.2–51.0 dB (prod 50.61 / 50.83) against a 50 dB threshold; the deterministic two-kernel version gets 52.29 / 52.70 | Accepted. FlyDSL reduces inside the kernel and reaches 52.4–52.6 dB | `AITER-5GEMM-STUDY.md` §2; `0915__opt/TWOKERNEL-SWEEP.md:76-78` |
| Clock behavior: the bar looks harder at low clocks, and ASM does not slow down at the training operating point | <ul><li>ASM fwd barely changes with sclk: 1.552 ms on A0 at 1100 MHz vs 1.396 ms on B0, only 1.11× apart (the same-code Triton bwd differs by 2.01×); on B0, 1350/2350 MHz gives 0.97×; after the reflash the clock is ×1.75 but ASM fwd is only 1.24× faster</li><li>PMC shows ASM fwd running at the power limit, with an effective in-kernel clock of only 1.36 GHz</li><li>Hence in the throttled era FlyDSL fwd looked 1.29–1.53× slower, but only 1.08× slower after the reflash</li><li>At the training operating point ASM stays essentially unchanged while FlyDSL slows down</li></ul> | Use same-process ratios only; added the gb ruler (measured after a GEMM burst); e2e is authoritative; reducing FlyDSL's clock sensitivity listed as a next step | `0915__repro__c07/RESULTS.md`; `0927__b0/profile/REPORT.md` §2.1; `0930__roofline/REPORT.md`; `1002__e2e/RESULT-realab.md` |
| Readings swing sharply with the launch pattern, and a ruler can make the wrong pick | <ul><li>Same-process ASM fwd: 1.438 ms for 20 back-to-back runs, 1.266 with a sync after each call, 1.225 when fwd is immediately followed by bwd; under profile, iso / blk / eburst / layer give 1.22 / 1.45 / 1.13 / 1.00 ms respectively</li><li>Per-call interleaved timing reads FlyDSL vs ASM systematically low: fwd by ~25%, bwd by ~3.4%</li><li>The fast shape is launch-bound (bwd beat 0.089 ms) and pulls the geometric mean up, which caused B0 bwd r27 to be misjudged as target_met</li></ul> | Switched to blocked timing (h40 / h66); the criterion changed to proxy and prod each ≥ beat (h69) | `0927__b0/e2e/BUILD.md` §3; `ruler/REPORT.md`; `OP-EVOLVE-SUGGESTIONS.md` #13 |
| The toolchain cannot see everything | <ul><li>llvm-objdump cannot decode TDM instructions (opcode 0x31, shown as `.long 0xd031…`), so ASM fwd's prefetch depth can only be inferred</li><li>rocprofv3's VGPR_Count shows only half (512, actually 1024)</li><li>gfx1250 .co files at one point "could not be disassembled"</li></ul> | <ul><li>Decode descriptors by dword; read `.vgpr_count` from the ISA</li><li>On 09-25 got the bwd disassembly with `--mcpu=gfx1250`: 864 v_wmma, 514 atomics, 26 barriers</li><li>After the 09-29 reflash ATT works and shows per-instruction stalls</li></ul> | `0925__flydsl/fwd-isa/REPORT.md`; `0930__bwd/notes/report_asm.md`; `AITER-5GEMM-STUDY.md` §1 |
| ASM itself is also far from the roofline (which means it can be beaten) | <ul><li>PMC: the bwd main kernel takes 7.71e6 cyc/SIMD, reaching only 34% of the 5-GEMM matrix lower bound</li><li>Only 1 wave per SIMD; per step, TDM waits cost 227 cyc, split barriers 208 cyc and atomic back-pressure 430 cyc</li><li>fwd reaches 72% of its lower bound, but the effective clock is only 1.36 GHz</li></ul> | s6 borrows the TDM 3-stage LDS ring and reads the next iteration's B operand into registers early; bwd reaches 103.8% of ASM | `0930__roofline/REPORT.md`; `0930__bwd/notes/report_asm.md`; `0930__bwd/REPORT.md` §2 |
| An undiagnosed card-state degradation | During T7 on 09-14, ASM fwd at one point lost its edge entirely (turbo only 13% slower); it has not reproduced since and was never diagnosed | Stability under training load has not been confirmed separately | `0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:128-130` |
| Wrong attributions circulating in the docs | <ul><li>"On B0, ASM bwd has no edge over fused (8.840 vs 8.848)" is wrong: both arms were fused Triton bwd; B0 never ran ASM bwd on 09-14</li><li>"A0/B0 clocks differ by 2.1× but attention only by 1.15×, so it is hardly clock-bound" holds only for ASM fwd: the bwd term compared A0's shim 10.160 with B0's fused 8.835</li><li>The 0914 `report.html` labeled the aiter Triton row as "hand-written ASM"</li></ul> | This section always relies on same-code, same-process ratios. Note that `skill: references/baselines.md` §8 **has not been corrected yet**: it still says "On B0 ASM bwd showed no edge over fused Triton (8.840 vs 8.848)" and treats B0's 8.835 as ASM bwd with shim overhead; it needs updating | `skill: references/baselines.md:272-293` (the erroneous original text); `0914__campaign/HANDOFF.md:436-450` (§15: the `--impl asm` and `--impl fused` arms were in fact the same path); `0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:103` (`--impl asm` = ASM forward + fused backward); `0915__repro__c07/RESULTS.md` |

**Summary**

1. **Op-level ranking**: multiples are comparable only within the same epoch, so they are given per epoch below; multiples from different epochs are not ranked on the same line.
   - bwd, E5 (A0 after the reflash, blocked, same-process): FlyDSL s6 0.963–0.972× (op-evolve job champion r24 ≈0.962× on the job ruler; 1.038× at the gb training operating point) ≈ aiter ASM > FlyDSL r29 1.20× (gb 1.342×). In the bench of the same period, factory-default main Triton dense is ~4.5× (cross-tool).
   - bwd, E1 (A0 throttled, tune_attention.py, all ≈ ratios against the E3 bar of 7.68 ms): in-tree vendored fused Triton plus ITT ≈2.30× (≈2.63× at `1cb2e183`) ≈ aiter Triton tuned champion ≈2.40× > flex ≈3.1× > aiter Triton factory default ≈3.2–3.6× > turbo Triton tuned ≈4.2× > turbo Triton factory default ≈5.9–6.4× > SDPA (only a fwd+bwd total of 97.063 ms).
   - The two groups are bridged by FlyDSL readings on throttled A0: r20 in E3 ≈1.39× and r29 in E3′ 1.44×, both faster than the best Triton in E1 (≈2.3×). The clock state is the same and the rulers differ, but the two differ by about 6–7 ms, far more than the ~1.4 ms of autograd overhead in the E1 ruler. The B0 E4 factory-default turbo ≈3.4× comes from a cross-process opcheck and is not merged into the ranking above.
   - fwd: aiter ASM > FlyDSL r13ns (E5 1.08×, 1.35× at the gb training operating point; E4 1.08×; E3′ 1.29×) > aiter FlyDSL m32x8 (E5 1.13×; E1/E3/E3′ 1.50–1.53×) > aiter Triton tuned > turbo Triton tuned > flex > turbo Triton factory default. In E2 (B0 at full clock) / E1 (A0 throttled) the last four are 1.49× / 2.09×, 1.94× / 2.66×, 2.67× / 4.8× and 3.7× / 6.8× respectively, in the same order in both epochs. aiter Triton factory default has two mutually contradictory readings in E1 (≈4.3× / ≈2.4×) and is left out of the ranking.
2. **e2e**: on A0 on 10-02, FlyDSL r16+s6 is on par with ASM (per step 0.999), and r16+r29 is 1.027 (same-process); on B0, the per-step time of factory-default turbo Triton is ~1.42× that of ASM (2,250 vs 1,582 ms, 14,563 vs 20,718 tps), but this is a cross-process ratio (turbo in a separate process, n7; ASM n5), not a same-process pairing like fly/asm. Before nkfix, differences between any attention backends were masked by GEMM.
3. **Why an in-house FlyDSL**: ASM is the fastest, but it is a black box — no Python entry point, a GQA out-of-bounds write, only 6 .co files for bwd, no varlen, non-deterministic dq, a conflict with the flydsl version, and it can be neither modified nor tuned. Triton is limited by its LDS and register structure and at best reaches about 2× ASM's time. aiter's FlyDSL has only fwd and is 1.5× slower (a same-process reading on throttled A0 when the project started on 09-17; 1.13× after the reflash). So on 09-17 the project was started on the grounds of "maintainable, adds varlen, deterministic, upstreamable".
4. **Result**: bwd has overtaken ASM (s6); fwd is still ~8% behind at op level and ~9 ms behind per step in e2e.

## (c) Progress of each optimization round

This section first gives the main line in a milestones-at-a-glance table (c.2a), then arranges every champion change point from 09-10 to 10-05 chronologically in a master table (c.2), and finally gives the current best versions (c.3) and the key points of the progress (c.4). Per-round details, one row per round, are in `rounds.xlsx` (198 rows, not merged; sheet 2 lists the rounds with an improvement separately). Paths without a prefix are relative to `Primus-Turbo/output/`; `OE:` stands for `/home/lihuzhan/code/2026_0910__op-evolve/op-evolve/artifacts/`.

### c.1 Measurement conventions
