Software stack: container fa-repro (image fa-tune:deps, i.e. amdprimus/amdprimus:gfx1250-20260910 plus the torchtitan dependencies), torch 2.11.0+rocm7.14.0a20260625, triton 3.6.0, aiter-src @6963ae9, torchtitan v0.2.2 (73a0e6979). Three flydsl versions coexist:
- 0.2.4: ships with the image; the product branch is pinned to it;
- 0.3.2: used by the A0 bwd job champion and the hand-campaign s1–s6;
- 0.3.4.1: used by the fwd job, B0 and e2e since 09-25.

**Rulers**
1. **tune_attention.py** (09-13..09-16): one layer of fwd+bwd, median of CUDA events, iters 20, a 256 MiB L2 flush every time; four-tensor SQNR gate (out/dq/dk/dv ≥50 dB). Reports fwd / bwd / total ms; TF/s is computed on the fwd+bwd total.
2. **op-evolve benchmark.py**, per-call interleaving (the two A0 jobs 09-17..09-27; B0 fwd r5–r9; B0 bwd r24–r26):
   - Each arm takes the median of 51 calls, with palindromic interleaving; the ASM beat and the candidate run in the same process.
   - Three shapes: fast (1,1024,1024,8,2,128), proxy (1,4096,4096,32,8,128), prod (4,8192,8192,32,8,128); **ranking is by prod only**.
   - gain = the ratio of this round's code to the best-ever code, both re-measured in the same session. min_gain: for bwd it was 0 at first (changed to 0.007 on B0 on 09-28 and on A0 on 09-30); for fwd it is 0.007.
   - From r9 on, the fwd job no longer runs in the same process as the beat (h28). After r10 was rejected on a three-shape arithmetic mean of 1.0022, the operator added gain_weights (prod 1 / proxy 0.25 / fast 0), effective from r11 [a8fa47bf]. The A0 bwd job also added gain_weights from r25 on, and fast is now scored by min.
3. **Blocked timing** (blocked, h40/h41/h66): B0 fwd from the act of r10 on (the act was already timed with blocked 9 + lead 4 at 09-28 03:16 UTC [0927__b0/fwd/rounds/010/act.md], whereas ruler/REPORT.md records the harness swap at ~03:40 UTC); B0 bwd from r27 on (r27 in progress.md already reports a blocked beat of 817.8, whereas ruler/bwd/REPORT.md records the install time as ~05:00 UTC). In both places the recorded install time is later than the actual use; the round is authoritative. A0 has used it ever since. Each arm first runs 4 untimed calls, then 9 consecutive timed calls, with palindromic order between passes. An audit found that per-call interleaving pushes the FlyDSL/ASM ratio down: by ~25% for fwd and ~3.4% for bwd. After switching to blocked timing, the A/A error is ±0.19% for fwd and ±0.05% for bwd.
4. **Real data and the training operating point** (h47): the inputs are 6 layers of real q/k/v dumped at B0 step 43. "gb" means running 10 bf16 32768×4096×14336 GEMMs before each timing to pull the clock down to the training operating point (~1.28 GHz on A0). FlyDSL is clock-sensitive and ASM almost insensitive, so the FlyDSL/ASM ratio can differ by more than 25% between the randn/blk and real/gb conditions.
5. **e2e**: Llama-3.1-8B BF16, 32 layers (some 09-15/16 experiments used 8 layers), MBS=GBS=4, seq 8192, 32,768 tokens per step, single card, no activation checkpointing, no compile; tps = tokens/s.
   - From 09-16 on, attention comparisons presuppose nkfix (rewriting the GEMM layout of aten::mm at runtime).
   - NKFIX_CHECK=1 costs ~33 ms extra per step (it was on in the 10-02 e2e and turned off in the 10-05 tutorial).
   - torchtitan's mfu column is computed against the A100 peak and is unusable.

**FLOP convention**
- Current convention (op-evolve `tools/op_flops.py`): fwd 2.199292e12, bwd 5.498229e12 (5-GEMM nominal count, causal taken as (s+1)/(2s)), fwd+bwd 7.697e12; ms = FLOP / TF/s. Values marked derived in the tables are all converted this way.
- TF/s in the 09-13..09-16 documents is the fwd+bwd total (7.697e12 / total ms). The 0913/0914 documents also reported bwd on a 7-GEMM issued basis (e.g. B0 vendored fused bwd 748.4 vs 534.6 TF/s); this section does not use that convention. The 09-14 B0 Triton op-evolve job counts bwd only.
- The 09-23 STAGE2-FWD-SWEEP uses fwd ≈2.233e12 (old harness; TF/s ~1.5% too high). The 5.8 TF/s of 09-17 DKDV-FIRST-TIMING is on a custom "useful" convention (single head) and is not comparable.
- Our 1-wave bwd (r0 through s6) actually issues 7 GEMMs, 1.4076× the algorithmic amount; aiter ASM issues only 1.0155×. This section always counts the algorithmic amount. So s6's "103.8% of ASM" is achieved while doing ~39% more matrix math.

**"% of ASM"**: ASM time ÷ this row's time (equal to this row's TF/s ÷ ASM TF/s). Same-process or same-session ASM measurements are preferred; above 100% means faster than ASM. Before 09-14 there was no ASM yet; recorded as "—". op-evolve rounds use that round's same-session beat.

**Why numbers are not comparable across epochs (measured examples)**
- **Same code, same ruler, only the platform changed** (A0-T 09-28 → A0-R 09-30):
  - FlyDSL fwd r13ns 2.023→1.353 ms (order o1; reversed order o2: 1.992→1.356), a 1.47–1.50× speedup (both orders, mean ~1.48×); ASM fwd 1.564→1.257 (1.24×; 1.23× for o2);
  - FlyDSL bwd r29 11.010→6.583 (1.67×), ASM bwd 7.654→5.503 (1.39×).
  - Even the ratio changed: bwd r29 went from 69.5% of ASM to 83.6%.
  - At the e2e level there is no clean like-for-like factor. The 1,947→1,351 ms (1.44×) for ASM attention + nkfix in tutorial §7 is not purely a platform difference: 1,947 comes from the void 09-28 a0_p3a process, in which the same-process FlyDSL arm was loaded from step 2 on and the fwd tree's `_env.py` then repointed `HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH` to the host library (whether hipBLASLt re-reads this variable mid-run is unproven); 1,351 is the 10-02 reading (NKFIX_CHECK=1 on both sides), and between the two runs the driver also changed from 7.1.1-2397345 to 7.1.0-2412954 (via -2410994, -2411826, -2411946) [0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md §2,§5.3,§6.3; 1002__e2e/E2E-PLAN.md §1].
- **Same machine and code, only the ruler changed**: after B0 fwd switched from interleaved to blocked, % of ASM jumped from ~85% to ~96%; the kernel did not change. From r16 on, the champion r13ns reads ~4.8% low on randn by design.
- **Same code, only the inputs and operating point changed**: A0 10-02, bwd s6/ASM time ratio blk 0.972, gb 1.038; fwd r16/ASM blk 1.081, gb 1.349.
- **Values drift across an AC cycle**: A0 09-22, the same r7 code, 404.06 vs 382.73 TF/s (+5.6%). Same-session noise floor 0.24–0.66%, cross-session drift ~1.5%.

**Round numbering**
- There are two sets of bwd rounds 24–27. One is r24–r32, run on B0 on 09-27/28, written "B0 r24" etc.; its ledgers are only in `0927__b0/bwd/`. The other is r24–r27, resumed on A0 in the live directory on 09-30 and 10-02, written "A0 r24" etc.

- The per-shape champions pointers in state.yaml are not necessarily the same as best_round. A0 bwd job: best_round=24, champions={prod: 25, proxy: 25, fast: 24}; r25, although rejected, holds the best-ever record for prod/proxy. A0 fwd job: best_round=11, champions={prod: 12, proxy: 11, fast: 11}. On resume, the readings of these rejected rounds are used as the best-ever threshold; this is how B0 fwd's r9 and r19 were blocked.

### c.2 Master table

Where a single row's "op time ms" is written as a / b / c, the values are fwd / bwd / total, in that order. Every number in the TF/s column is followed by its convention: "(total)" = 7.697e12 ÷ total fwd+bwd ms, which is how tune_attention and the Primus bench report it, and it is used throughout Stages 1–2 except #13; "(bwd)" and "(fwd)" are per direction, computed with 5.498229e12 and 2.199292e12 respectively, and the B0 Triton job in #13 and everything from Stage 3 on are per direction. Numbers on the total convention cannot be compared directly with per-direction numbers. Consecutive rejected rounds are merged into one row; per-round details are in rounds.xlsx.

### c.4 Key points of the progress curve

- **The top e2e lever is GEMM, not attention.**
  - The hipBLASLt path fix took A0 32L from 244 to 2,027 tps (8.3×). nkfix then brought another 6.16× on A0 8L; on B0 32L the ASM arm reached 20,718 tps, 10.3× the pre-fix high tier of 2,016–2,022 tps (10.8× vs the low-tier median 1,922), with GEMM per step going 15.6–16.5 s→0.83–0.86 s, ~18–20×.
  - Before the fix, attention was only <1% to 3.3% of a step: on A0 09-13 32L via rocBLAS, the whole attention path was 35.7 ms × 32 = 1.14 s, ~0.85% (phase1/RESULTS.md:266); A0 8L via hipBLASLt without nkfix, 3.3% (BOTTLENECK-SHIFT); B0 09-28 before nkfix, 1.4–5.4% (0927__b0/e2e/RESULT.md:37). The e2e A/B of ASM bwd was therefore drowned in e2e noise (8L n=9, −0.28%, not significant). After the fix attention takes 15–22% (A0 8L 20.8%, B0 17–22%, A0-D 10-02 ~15%), and only then do op-level improvements become visible in e2e.
- **Triton stage: 2.44× in one day on A0, 2.75× in one day on B0.**
  - On A0-T 59.642→24.435 ms, from two config knobs plus the vendored aiter fused bwd.
  - On B0-14, first reproduced the Day-1 ladder (28.121→12.994 ms), then got down to 10.235 ms with waves_per_eu, ASM fwd (fwd 1.82×) and in-thread transpose: 2.75× vs factory default.
  - waves_per_eu shows no measurable gain on the throttled machine but is worth 4.2% at full clock: the machine state itself decides which levers work.
- **ASM fwd is the largest single jump in the fwd direction** (A0 4.253→1.549 ms, 2.74×) and has served as the bar ever since. The ASM bwd bar was at first overstated in time by 32% by a bogus 10.160 ms shim number (equivalent to understating ASM's speed); the 09-24 census set it at 7.6766 ms, and FlyDSL's "0.92×" was corrected to "0.70–0.72×" accordingly.
- **FlyDSL bwd from scratch to ~0.71×** (A0-T, 20 rounds, 57.24→511.42 TF/s, 8.9×; 0.714 vs the 09-24 bar of 716 TF/s, 0.72 using r22's same-session re-measurement of 516.87).
  - Comparing prod-champion TF/s round over round (each round's reading in its own session), the four biggest jumps are r1 1.62× (57.24→92.62, causal tile skipping), r4 1.60× (207.47→330.98, LDS padding removing 64-way bank conflicts), r2 1.55× (address clamping + 32-deep contraction + BLOCK_KV 32) and r3 1.45× (k_dq BLOCK_Q 64).
  - The framework's three-shape gain ranks them differently: r2 1.921, r4 1.512, r13 1.370, r1 1.342, r3 1.297. r13's gain came entirely from fast/proxy; prod did not move.
  - After r12 came the efficiency phase, with the ceiling capped by the 7 vs 5 GEMM structural factor of 1.386×; r13–r23 added only ~2–3% on prod.
- **fwd gains come mainly from scheduling and softmax instructions.**
  - On A0-T 935→1111 TF/s (+18.8%), made up of three items: longest-first scheduling, packed exp/row-sum, and nodelay.
  - On B0, speculative softmax gave +4.6% on randn but was actually slower on real data and at the training clock. After r16 turned speculation off (r13ns), real data got 8–15% faster, and real data plus the training clock 12–23% faster. The same change goes in opposite directions on the two rulers.
- **Corrections to rulers and criteria themselves "rewrote" part of the progress.**
  - After B0 switched from interleaved to blocked, fwd % of ASM went from ~85% to ~96%. At the same time two false wins were retracted (lab2-L12 +6.4%→−0.4%, lab3-L21 +6.3%→~+0.3%), and fwd r6's +5.7% was revised down to +4.6% (still a real gain).
  - r15's arithmetic mean, B0 r27's geomean falsely flagging target_met, and A0 r24's fast-median false positive: all three framework criterion errors were corrected by hand.
- **The A0 firmware reflash made the same code 1.24–1.67× faster (o1 order; in the reverse order o2, ASM fwd 1.23× and FlyDSL fwd 1.47×), with FlyDSL benefiting more**: bwd r29 rose from 69.5% to 83.6% of ASM, and fwd r13ns/ASM fell from 1.29 to 1.08. All A0 absolute numbers from before 09-29 are void. e2e has no clean same-convention ratio (the 1.44× is not pure, see c.1).
- **The bwd hand campaign went from 83.6% to 103.8% in one day** (09-30, r29 6.58→s6 5.295 ms, −19.5%).
  - The core lever is the 3-stage TDM LDS ring, plus reading the next iteration's B operands back into registers early. Both steps are whole-op changes: swapping in dkdv_tdm3 for k_dkdv alone made the whole op −11.17% vs r29 (5.856 vs 6.592 ms); after k_dqg also moved to TDM (s5), the whole op went a further −2.1% vs s4. Ablation A2 first gave the ceiling, then ATT (available only after the reflash) pinned it down to specific instructions.
  - s6 still has the 7-GEMM structure, doing ~39% more matrix math, and is still faster than ASM.
  - The 5-GEMM fused version w4f needs only 4.241 ms without dQ atomic accumulation (relax_noatom) (129.6% of ASM; output wrong, ceiling only), but dQ atomic writes eat ~5 ms, so it is paused.
- **e2e went from 4.8–5.0% behind to parity.**
  - On B0 fly/asm 1.0475 (r6+r20)→1.0454 (swap in fwd r13, still speculative)→1.0403 (swap in fwd r13ns)→1.0323 (swap in bwd r29). On A0-D, switching further to s6 gives 0.999, saving 53 ms of bwd per step. Absolute ms on B0 and A0-D are not comparable; only same-process ratios are compared here.
  - The remaining gap is in fwd: r16/ASM 1.349 at the training operating point, 9.2 ms slower per step.
  - The product branch is still on r29; s6 has not been ported into it yet. The same combination (fwd r16 + bwd r29) was 1.027× of ASM in #81, but that was a flydsl 0.3.4.1 build; the branch itself (0.2.4) has only been measured in the tutorial e2e at 1,361.9 ms/step, which is NKFIX_CHECK=0; converted to the same convention with +33 ms/step it is ~3% slower than ASM (estimated, not measured). Next steps are fwd, and switching the scoring ruler to the training-operating-point gb ruler (designed 10-02, not installed).

## (d) Problems encountered along the way and their solutions

**Reading conventions**

- Machines and clock states (absolute numbers from different states cannot be compared directly; wherever they appear side by side, this is stated separately):
  - **A0-throttled**: heliosr-1b114-c07-1 (single card), before the 2026-09-29 firmware reflash; VR throttle, DPM has only two levels, 500/1100 MHz; 1100 MHz idle, ~0.95–1.07 GHz under load.
  - **A0-new-firmware**: the same card, after the 09-29 reflash of VBIOS 630A→700E and SMU 125.7.1→125.12.0. 1.74–2.03 GHz at op level, ~1.5 GHz during training (2,500 W power limit). From 10-01 the driver is amdgpu-dkms 7.1.0-2412954.
  - **B0**: ctheliosp-1b112-a37-1, 4×gfx1250, 2,133–2,244 MHz under load. ~1.25–1.69 GHz during training after the 09-28 nkfix.
- How sources are written:
  - Paths omit the prefix `output/`.
  - `s:<first 8 chars of session id> <time Z>` refers to a session transcript.
  - `OE:` refers to the op-evolve repository `/home/lihuzhan/code/2026_0910__op-evolve/op-evolve`.
  - `skill:` refers to `.claude/skills/gfx1250-attn-campaign/references/*.md` or `~/.claude/skills/*`.
  - An 8-digit hex string is a Primus-Turbo commit.
  - `hNN` is a hint number: A0 bwd in `0923__flydsl/hint.md`, A0 fwd in `0925__flydsl/fwd-job/hint.md`, B0 in `0927__b0/{fwd,bwd}-hint.md`, and A0 after 09-30 (h83/h85) in the bwd job's `job_context/hint.md`.
- All times are UTC. There are two sets of bwd round numbers r24–r27: one on B0 09-27/28 and one on A0 09-30/10-02. This section always states the machine.

---

### d.1 GPU wedges and AC power cycles

On both machines, once a card is wedged for good (a wedge), no software means can bring it back: the driver's own reset cannot complete, the container holds unkillable D-state processes, and the reference count of `/dev/kfd` never drops to zero. The only way to recover is for the user to go to the machine in person and cut power to the whole box (an AC power cycle). Wedges are therefore first a scheduling constraint, and only second a technical problem.

#### d.1.1 Incident table (deduplicated across sources)

Class legend:

- **A**: out-of-bounds address write by a reference GEMM (fp32 Tensile), i.e. the "memory out-of-bounds" class.
- **B**: TLB/queue class. The first line is an `INVALIDATE_TLBS` timeout, with no memory fault of any kind before it.
- **C**: an out-of-bounds read by a candidate kernel sets off a fault storm, accompanied by `IH ring buffer overflow`.
- **S**: startup-time MES fault family, unattributed.
- **K**: known operational triggers, including online tuning, inductor, bad candidates, the profiler, and new kernels sent straight to prod.
- **R**: resource or process problems, such as SIGBUS, leftover KFD state, or something holding the card.
- **F**: process-level fault, recoverable, not counted as a wedge.
- **D**: degraded, not wedged for good.
- **X**: non-kernel causes, such as the driver, firmware, or coordination with others.

| Date (UTC) | Machine | What was running | Trigger / root cause | dmesg signature | Class | Recovery | Cost | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

There were also several survivable faults; they did not need an AC, but each of them was misjudged at the time:

- The out-of-bounds read during the first attempt of r1 on 09-17, PF 0x3 RW 0x0.
- The batch at ~14:15 on 09-23, and the faults at shape switches on 09-24 at 03:58, 07:58 and 10:51.

All of these cost only the process. Sources: s:c4b79aa6 09-17; 0e9ca37c; `0924__flydsl/DAY-SUMMARY.md:84-87`.

#### d.1.2 Wedge classes and how our understanding evolved

1. **09-04..09-13 (A0-throttled): first treated as a platform problem.**
   - Four unrelated workloads all triggered the same kind of wedge: MES unresponsive, reset unable to complete. The four workloads were HipKittens GEMM, grouped-GEMM, the E3 mask outer product, and E4 PC sampling.
   - The conclusions at the time: the card itself is fragile; PC sampling always wedges; reloading the driver is not feasible, because D-state processes do not release `/dev/kfd` and the amdgpu refcount never drops to zero.
   - It was also found that during a wedge, `rocm-smi`, `ps -eo …wchan`, `pgrep` and `docker exec` all hang themselves.
   - Action: filed HARDWARE-ISSUE / PLATFORM-ESCALATION and moved to B0 on 09-14.
2. **09-14..09-17: learned to tell three states apart.**
   - "Degraded" is not "wedged for good". B0 GPU0 had 120 `MES failed to respond` lines but could still run kernels to completion. Only `wait for reset ack`, `ring gfx timeout` and `GPU reset begin` mean unrecoverable.
   - A "process-level fault" is not a "wedge". The `Queues reset on process` on 09-17 did not need an AC.
   - There were many startup wedges during e2e work: on 09-16, 3 of the day's 14 launches wedged (21%), from which it followed that **risk is priced by the number of launches, not by run duration**.
   - Confirmed triggers identified: autotune inside the training step, `converters: []`→inductor, Triton `num_warps=16`, bad candidate #58, starting a new run while KFD leftovers remain, `modprobe -r`, repeated SIGKILL of running kernels, and a "CPU-only" agent going onto the card on its own.
   - Another conclusion: SIGBUS is a symptom, not the cause.
3. **09-21..09-24 (op-evolve bwd job): split into three classes A/B/C by the first dmesg line; the discriminants were falsified one by one.**
   - Four "wedge precursor" discriminants were proposed in turn, and all were overturned by measurement:
     - h15: truncated sub-4GB address + PF 0x5/RW 0x1 + `copy_context_work_handler`. Class B wedges never trigger it at all.
     - h22: `IH ring buffer overflow`. During the 09-24 boot there were 8 overflows and the card stayed alive, while the 08:58 wedge had no overflow at all.
     - MORE_FAULTS / multiple XCDs. 8/8 of the survivable batches had MORE_FAULTS.
     - A fault arrival rate of "6000×". That number is actually the driver's ~97 ms throttling (7436cd29).
   - Conclusion (3fd48059): **there is no usable wedge predictor**; alert only on the wedge itself.
   - Two triggers were actually pinned down in this period: first, the fp32 Tensile reference that the gate ran on the card, solved with refcache; second, running multiple shapes in a single process, which faulted in ~44% of processes; after switching to one shape per process, 6 consecutive runs had 0 faults.
   - In the same period it was also found that `dmesg_restrict=1` had invalidated all earlier "zero faults" checks.
4. **09-28..10-06: the host library and new kernels became the main triggers; the firmware reflash did not make the card any sturdier.**
   - The host hipBLASLt library `~/.local/hipblaslt-gfx1250` is associated with several incidents: on A0 on 09-28, one NaN and one wedge; B0 stuck at step 1; the 10-02 wedge.
   - Running the fp32 reference GEMM on the card still causes faults (B0 GPU2).
   - After the 09-29 reflash the clock recovered and ATT became usable, but on 10-02 a new kernel sent straight to prod still wedged the card. On 10-06, `hipblaslt-bench` wedged it too when its enumeration hit a bad Tensile solution.
   - The rules converged into h85: scale new kernels up step by step from toy→prod; probes that only read ISA/VGPR are always compile-only; use only the hipBLASLt library shipped in the image.
   - Another data point: B0 had ≥12 training launches on 09-27/28 with 0 wedges. Pooling all periods, the startup wedge rate is about ≤5/31≈16% (`1002__e2e/E2E-PLAN.md:377`).

#### d.1.3 Countermeasures that worked

| Measure | Failure addressed | Introduced | Evidence / effect | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| No driver-level recovery: `modprobe -r amdgpu` removed from the recovery ladder. On a wedge, do only a single time-limited read of `timeout 20 sudo -n dmesg` (then tail) and of a bounded `/sys`, send no signals to D-state processes, write the `.stop` sentinel, then switch to CPU work | 09-15 whole machine lost; repeated kills left more unkillable processes | Since 09-15 | No incident has been made worse by recovery actions since then | 457593d8; skill:`gfx1250-card-safety` §4–§5 |
| Price risk per launch: merge several configs into one process; use a 60-step long run instead of several short runs; between two runs, wait for KFD to drain, then cool down 25–45 s; never run the fwd/bwd jobs in parallel on one card; one flock lock per card | Startup family (21%/launch) | Since 09-16 | B0: ≥12 launches, 0 wedges; 10-02 e2e: 0 faults | skill:`gfx1250-card-safety` §0; `1002__e2e/E2E-PLAN.md` |
| Compile-only first (`COMPILE_ONLY=1 ARCH=gfx1250 FLYDSL_GPU_ARCH=gfx1250 HIP_VISIBLE_DEVICES=-1`); any spill>0 or `private_segment_fixed_size>0` is eliminated outright | A spilling build hangs on its first launch (costing one power cycle) | 09-21 (b1572877) | VGPR ≤951 never spills; =1024 always spills | skill:`env-and-pitfalls` §2b §6 |
| New address arithmetic is first proven in-bounds on the CPU, by full enumeration rather than sampling | Class C out-of-bounds reads | 09-23..09-24 | k_dq: full enumeration of 134,217,728 tuples, conclusion: a bijection; 3 latent defects found in addition (see d.6) | 41c916b7; h22/h33 |
| Order for putting a new kernel on the card: toy shape → separate process → `AMD_SERIALIZE_KERNEL=3` → no in-process autotune → through the lock/KFD wrapper → image hipBLASLt only. h85 must be written as a hint table row | 10-02 wedge | In the skill since 09-17; upgraded to h85 on 10-02 | 0 wedges during the 09-30 hand campaign s1–s6 (w4f's first time on the card used toy only, nsp=1, serial single process) | 1ee0dd59; skill:`gfx1250-card-safety` §1 #13, §2 |
| One shape per process; abort at the first rc≠0 | 09-24 wedge; shape-boundary faults at ~44%/process | 09-24 (bf58bec0) | 6 full measurements with 0 faults afterwards | `0924__flydsl/wedge-rootcause/` |
| Reference values moved off the GPU (refcache), and only the image hipBLASLt library used | Class A wedges | 09-22 (376b9fd3 and others), 10-02 (h85) | The prod reference FAULTs outright on the card; computed on the CPU instead, 10m26s, producing 1.1 GB | 376b9fd3 (commit message); `0922__flydsl/gate-patch/README.md` |
| Disable PC sampling | 09-11 wedge; 09-24 near-wedge | Since 09-11; reaffirmed 09-24 (82420bdf) | On the old firmware, all 3 attempts faulted: 1 wedge (09-11 10:48), 2 with the card surviving (09-11 10:32, 09-24 ~05:24). `0930__bwd/PLAN.md` D2 and skill:`gfx1250-attn-campaign/SKILL.md:106` say "3/3 wedged", which overstates it. After the reflash, rocprofv3 1.3.2 rejects this configuration outright at startup | skill:`env-and-pitfalls` §2c; OE:`output/0911__fa_gfx1250_phase1/HARDWARE-ISSUE.md` §2.3; 82420bdf |
| Kill processes only by explicit PID, never with `pkill -f` (it matches itself and has killed our own shell 4+ times); start long loops with `setsid nohup`, stop them with `op-evolve stop` or `kill -TERM -<PGID>`; bind watchdogs to `E2E_RUN_MARKER` or a PID; periodically check for orphans with ppid=1 | Wrong kills, orphans holding the card, monitors watching the wrong run | 09-14..09-25 | 6ad9bda0 (reap by process group); 6029b430 | skill:`gfx1250-card-safety` §6; `op-evolve-ops` §3 |
| Monitoring and guarding: `patrol.sh` wedge alerts now latch and are checked against the boot time (617481d7); `release_guard` writes a pidfile and is started by patrol, with new "supervisor stuck in backoff" and "state.yaml frozen" checks (dd1aec29, 4547915c); `card_ok.sh` looks at KFD holders and `/sys` VRAM and runs one toy matmul with a hard timeout (ac95f1c7); a root-free liveness probe `timeout 20 docker exec fa-repro true`; alert only on MES failed/unrecoverable and docker exec timeouts | Alerts fired only once; the guard daemon was never started; the health script falsely reported HEALTHY | 09-21..09-24 | Since 09-21, every wedge has been reported within one patrol cycle | 617481d7; 3fd48059 |
| Online tuning replaced by an offline-built table; bad candidates recorded as a terminal state | Autotune inside the training step; sweep candidates wedging the card | 09-16 | `flydsl_table.py`: one subprocess per shape + hard timeout + SQNR gate; `num_warps=16` and #58 permanently excluded | f08ccffe; e4730158 |
| e2e defenses: `converters: []` banned; `compile.enable: false`; keep VRAM headroom (8L or `E2E_MEM_STOP=89.5`); run ASM for the first two steps; BLAS guard (exit 94); non-finite-value watchdog (exit 96); foreign KFD processes (exit 91) | The e2e wedges on 09-15/16 and 09-28 | 09-15..10-02 | 10-02 e2e: two 92-step processes, 0 faults | `1002__e2e/E2E-PLAN.md` §3 |
| When delegating tasks, explicitly forbid touching the card, and check the KFD holders before and after delegating | 09-16 agent using the card on its own | 09-16 | No unauthorized card use since | 4e03bd57 |
| Push at the end of every round; run `git fsck --no-dangling` after every AC | Power cuts truncating git objects (see d.7) | 09-23 07:36 | The 09-23 09:42 AC did not corrupt the repository | memory `push-after-every-round` |

**Recovery steps after an AC** (gfx1250-card-safety §5, `1002__oe/incident/WEDGE-1002.md`):

```bash
# 0. The user does the AC power cycle (only this step recovers a wedge; never modprobe -r)
sudo modprobe amdgpu                    # kernel cmdline has modprobe.blacklist=amdgpu, so it does not load automatically
sleep 6
sudo sysctl -w kernel.dmesg_restrict=0  # reverts to 1 on every reboot; confirm dmesg | wc -l shows thousands of lines
cat /sys/class/drm/card*/device/pp_dpm_sclk   # check whether someone changed the DPM table/VBIOS/dkms again
ls /sys/class/kfd/kfd/proc/             # must be empty
docker start fa-repro                   # reuse, do not rebuild; confirm the container has no image-shipped primus_turbo
# one 4096^3 bf16 matmul with a timeout (A0-throttled ~5.07 ms) -- this is what counts as "the card works", rocm-smi does not
git fsck --no-dangling; find .git/objects -size 0   # a power cut may truncate git objects
# add the hint first (e.g. h85), then op-evolve resume (without --config)
```

#### d.2.2 What nkfix is and how to install it

- **Problem**: the hipBLASLt in the image ships pure-bf16 tuned libraries only for the TN layout (`Alik_Bljk`) used by the forward Linear. The backward dgrad (`Ailk_Bljk`) and wgrad (`Ailk_Bjlk`) can only look up the sparse GridBased table, whose nearest neighbor lands at N=1, so they end up with the GEMV tile `MT32x16x32` at only 50–80 TF/s. The forward reaches 1.5–1.9 PF/s, an 11–32× gap per call.
- **Approach**: intercept `aten::mm` with `TorchDispatchMode` and change the physical layout of the backward GEMM operands to the forward one, so that they hit `MT256x256x128`:
  - dgrad, when B is contiguous, becomes `mm(A, B.t().contiguous().t())`.
  - wgrad becomes `mm(A.contiguous(), B.t().contiguous().t())`. The wgrad criterion must be checked before the dgrad one.
- **Approaches that did not work**:
  - The zero-copy form `(BᵀAᵀ)ᵀ` still lands on the bad tile, only 0.83–0.95×, because this library has good tiles for the TN layout only.
  - Overriding `aten::mm` with `torch.library` recurses infinitely.
  - Putting it in `sitecustomize` fails because torch is not loaded yet at that point.
- **Numerics**: op-level SQNR is identical to the original call, 55.59–55.63 dB in both cases; over 99.996% of elements differ by at most 1 ulp; bitwise deterministic across 3 repeats.
- **Versions**:
  - A0 v1 (09-15) changes dgrad only.
  - A0 v3 (09-16) adds wgrad; rule 3 is the FlyDSL wgrad, driven by an offline table and off by default.
  - B0 `nkfix_b0.py` (09-28) is a rewrite on top of 1255557f:
    - Persistent scratch, keyed by (device, stream).
    - Chunked along the output dimension, chunk size `NKFIX_CHUNK_BYTES` defaulting to 256 MiB; the K reduction is not split.
    - Transposes use the blocked Triton kernel `transpose_triton.py`, bitwise identical to the torch copy and 5–7× faster.
    - `NKFIX_CHECK=0/1/2` checks for non-finite values; `NKFIX_SHADOW` runs a shadow comparison during real training.
  - The tutorial uses exactly this B0 version.
- **Installation**:
  - Tutorial route: `primus-nkfix-hook.patch` adds 14 lines to `_run_trainer_lifecycle` in Primus's `primus/core/runtime/train_runtime.py`, which import nkfix and call `install()` when `NKFIX_ENABLE=1`.
  - B0 e2e kit route: the shim's `primus_turbo/__init__.py`, together with `E2E_NKFIX=1`.
  - The Primus-side hook from A0 09-15/16 is backed up only in `0915__opt/primus-side/` and was never pushed.
  - `transpose_triton.py` must be placed in the same directory as `nkfix.py`.
- **Overhead**: ~70 ms per step; `NKFIX_CHECK=1` adds another ~33 ms/step.

#### d.2.4 NaN, `HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH` and host-library risks

1. **nkfix and NaN (A0-throttled, 09-16)**
   - Runs with nkfix hit NaN in 8/39 cases, runs without in 0/35 (p=0.004). A separate count: with nkfix, 7 of 40 runs showed only `grad_norm=inf`; without, 3 of 36.
   - All NaNs occurred at step ≤10 and were entangled with that day's MES TLB faults. The root cause was never found.
   - The B0 rewrite: 8 nkfix runs in total, 486 steps (150 unstable steps), 0 NaN. The upper bounds are computed only over the 7 runs at 32 layers among them, 466 steps (138 unstable steps): the 95% upper bound per step is 2.2%/step; per run it is 35%/run, not yet enough to rule out A0's 21%/run; pushing it below 21% would take ~13 clean runs (`0927__b0/gemm/REPORT.md` §0 §4).
   - Hence nkfix stays opt-in; turn on `NKFIX_CHECK=1` for long runs or debugging; any NaN or inf voids that run's speed data in its entirety.
2. **Library path**
   - The default search path is one directory level too high: `library/` contains only a single `gfx1250/` subdirectory, which holds all 402 files, so the result is `INVALID_VALUE`. The 09-23 S0-b directory table did not list this subdirectory, which is why the host library was adopted instead (see d.2.1).
   - `_rocm_sdk_devel/.../library/gfx1250` is an incomplete copy (326 files, no `Kernels.so-000`, no extop/transform) and gives SIGSEGV, exit 139.
   - `/opt/rocm/lib/hipblaslt` is an empty directory; the container's ROCm is not under `/opt/rocm` at all.
   - Falling back to rocBLAS makes e2e 8× slower, and its fp32 Tensile kernel has also caused page faults on the card.
3. **`TORCH_BLAS_PREFER_HIPBLASLT` is silently rewritten, in at least three places**
   - `sitecustomize.py` uses `setdefault("0")`; we added this ourselves on 09-17.
   - `/etc/profile.d/zz-gfx1250.sh` exports it as 0; this only takes effect in a login shell, so `bash -lc` gets caught.
   - `tune_attention.py` sets it to 0 itself before importing torch.
   - Rules: assign only, never `setdefault`; use `bash -c`; confirm by printing `torch.backends.cuda.preferred_blas_library()` inside the process.
   - Also, `runtime.env` in the op-evolve spec does not actually take effect; the setting must go into the op's own code or the exec command line.
4. **Host library `~/.local/hipblaslt-gfx1250` (a copy from host ROCm 10.1.0, 328 files)**
   - Validated only on square matrices.
   - Associated with these incidents: the B0 hang at step 1 (p1a/p1b single-variable control, n=1 per side); GEMM bursts ~20× slower; the A0 09-28 NaN and wedge; the 10-02 wedge. Apart from p1a/p1b, all of this is correlational evidence only.
   - Banned by h85 since 10-02.
   - `skill:env-and-pitfalls` §1b still recommends the host library to this day; it is outdated and needs updating.
5. **fp32 GEMM reference on the card**: causes class-A wedges (refcache) and the B0 GPU2 fault (h50). References for tests and gates are always computed on the CPU or served from refcache; a refcache sha mismatch is an immediate FAIL (see d.5).
6. **`hipblaslt-bench --algo_method all`**: hits bad precompiled solutions and wedges the card; do not use it.

#### d.2.5 Currently recommended configuration (10-05 tutorial, measured on A0-new-firmware)

```bash
# Patch the nkfix hook into Primus: git apply docs/gfx1250_llama31_8b_e2e/primus-nkfix-hook.patch
# Inside the container: pip uninstall -y primus_turbo (the editable install in the image shadows the checkout); do not use REBUILD_PRIMUS_TURBO=1
docker exec -w $PWD fa-repro bash -c '            # bash -c, not bash -lc
export TORCH_BLAS_PREFER_HIPBLASLT=1              # explicit assignment, never setdefault
export HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH=/opt/venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/_rocm_sdk_libraries_gfx1250/lib/hipblaslt/library/gfx1250   # image library only
export NKFIX_ENABLE=1 NKFIX_CHECK=0               # NKFIX_CHECK=1 for long runs / NaN debugging (+~33 ms/step)
export PYTHONPATH=$TURBO/docs/gfx1250_llama31_8b_e2e/nkfix:$TURBO   # nkfix.py and transpose_triton.py in the same directory
...'
```

- Checks:
  - The log must contain `[nkfix] installed` and must not contain `[nkfix] triton transpose unavailable`.
  - Steady state is ~24k tps (A0-new-firmware). Settling at ~19.4k means `transpose_triton.py` is missing; only ~2k means nkfix is not in effect.
- yaml config: `flavor: 8B_flex`, `converters: ["primus_turbo"]`, `use_turbo_attention: true`, `compile.enable: false`, and set `debug.seed`.
- The real fix has to come from the image side: add bf16 tuned libraries for NN/wgrad and fix the library path (`0915__opt/VENDOR-REPORT-hipblaslt.md`).

---

### d.3 Timing and ruler problems

| Problem | Symptom | Root cause | Solution | Cost/lesson | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| The ASM bwd reference of 10.160 ms was a shim artifact | From 09-15..09-24, 10.160 ms / 541 TF/s was consistently treated as the ASM bwd reference (A0-throttled), so FlyDSL bwd was reported as 0.92× of ASM | The 09-15 measurement was of the `_AsmFwdAsmBwd` autograd shim, which called `asm_backward` without passing `hip=`/`scratch=`, so every call did 3 hipModuleLoad calls and freshly allocated ~1 GiB of scratch, and the autograd pipeline was counted as well. The corrected path (8.13–8.68 ms) was available the same day but did not propagate; the number was also mislabeled as "product path" | Switched to a same-process census with the op-evolve beat: 7.6766 ms (n=224 raw) / 7.6769 (n=146 deduplicated), ~716 TF/s. The n=177/7.6721 version has also been retracted. Rule: quote only ratios against the same-process beat | Progress was overstated: 0.92× was actually 0.70–0.72×. 11.726 ms/layer, 4.76× and 1.74× are void as well. The overhead breakdown (+1.15 / +1.42 / +0.01 ms) exists only as a prose description, so only the total can be quoted | skill:`baselines.md` §0 §5; 77963d7e; `0924__flydsl/DAY-SUMMARY.md` |
| Per-call interleaved timing vs. blocked timing (B0, 09-28) | For the same code, merely moving its position or changing the combination of same-process arms shifts the reading by 2–10%. r6/beat measured 1.29× interleaved and 1.035× at steady state; bwd beat/current measured 0.7618 interleaved and 0.7878 blocked | Every call inherits the power/clock state left behind by the previous arm, which even a 2 GB flush does not clear, so the result favors the higher-power arm | Switched to blocked timing: each arm first runs 4 calls, then times 9, in palindromic order (fwd from 03:40 via h40/h41, bwd from ~05:00 via h66). Every ranking process carries an A/A copy (fwd ±0.19%, bwd ±0.05%); if it deviates by more than 0.5%, the whole process is void (h35) | Three false wins: r6 reported +5.7% (actually +4.6%); L12 reported +6.4% (actually −0.4%); L21 reported +6.3% (actually +0.3–0.6%; the two documents use different conventions, both at the edge of noise). %ASM before r6 was systematically low (fwd by ~25%, bwd by ~3.4%). Absolute ms from the old and new harnesses cannot be compared | `0927__b0/ruler/REPORT.md`; `0927__b0/ruler/bwd/REPORT.md`; `0927__b0/REPORT-0928.html` §6 |
| I-cache penalty after ASM (A0-throttled fwd job, 09-25..27) | Running aiter ASM first and then the FlyDSL kernel in the same process takes I$ misses from 0 to 808 and adds a fixed 25–42 µs to the cold start; fast/proxy medians drift with the arm's position | The beat and the candidates were arranged palindromically in the same process (A B C C B A), and the arm right next to the beat is penalized. This is unrelated to power, loop count or code size. After the B0 audit, the same kind of phenomenon was explained as "state inheritance"; both explanations lead to the same fix | h28: compare candidates and the champion in processes without the beat, rotating over at least 3 sessions; measure the beat in a separate process | The verdicts of r3/r6/r8 were all affected; r8 nodelay, with proxy 0.956 (1.004 with the beat removed), was wrongly rejected | `0925__flydsl/fwd-job/hint.md` h28; skill:`fwd.md` |
| randn inputs or real data (B0 09-28; A0 10-02 re-check) | B0: r6 speculative softmax is 4.6% faster on randn; but in training, FlyDSL fwd (r6) vs. ASM per layer ranges from 1.19 (layer 0) to 1.72 (layer 7), and it gets slower as training goes on (50.2→55.3→59.2 ms/step). At op level with real inputs, at the clock right after a GEMM, it is 1.45–1.67; the same data under the blocked ruler gives 1.15–1.30, and randn blocked gives 1.02 | The score standard deviation of real q/k/v is 21–53 (randn ~1), and 13–25% of tile steps trigger a recompute (randn: 0%) | Use the 6-layer real dumps from step 43 (`/home/lihuzhan/_prof_dump/qkv_call0*.pt`) as a second ruler (h45/h47); from r18 on, both rulers are reported. r13ns, with speculation turned off, was adopted via refactor h44: 4.8% slower on randn, 12–23% faster on real data plus training clock | From fwd r16 on, the job's randn readings are by design ~4.8% low, so %ASM before and after r16 cannot be compared. On A0 on 09-30, s1–s6 were measured on randn only (at the time the dumps were believed to exist only on B0, see d.7). On 10-02 a follow-up measurement on A0-new-firmware with the same real dumps: real data agrees with randn (bwd s6/ASM blk 0.972, randn 0.957; fwd r16/ASM 1.081, randn 1.080), so the r16/s6 conclusions hold on real inputs. What opens the gap is the operating point: under gb, ASM barely moves while FlyDSL slows down in both directions; bwd goes from 2.8% ahead to 3.8% behind, and fwd from 8% behind to 35% behind (see the next row) | `0927__b0/profile/REPORT.md` §0 §1 §2.2; `0927__b0/fwd-nospec/REPORT.md`; `1002__e2e/RESULT-realab.md` |
| Clock at the training operating point (gb ruler) | During B0 training, attention runs at 1,250–1,690 MHz (idle 2,356), with the whole board at ~2.13 kW. Time ratios at 1350/2350 MHz fitted on B0: FlyDSL fwd r6 1.25 (r²=0.28); ASM fwd 0.97 (r²=0.01), essentially independent of sclk. A0-new-firmware 10-02 (real data), ratios under the three rulers (blk / gb / e2e): fwd r16/ASM 1.081 / 1.349 / 1.263; bwd s6/ASM 0.972 / 1.038 / 0.974; bwd r29/ASM 1.206 / 1.341 / 1.290; s6/r29 0.806 / 0.774 / 0.754 | On A0-new-firmware, the blk ruler's call window is ~1.45–1.5 GHz; the gb ruler is designed for the "GEMM just finished" operating point, with a call window of ~1.26–1.39 GHz (realab records ~1.28 GHz); the A0 e2e whole-step sclk median is ~1.50 GHz. The clock in the attention window during training has never been measured on A0; 1,250–1,690 MHz is the B0 reading. Under the power limit, time also depends on the data: on A0, r24 with all-zero operands takes 34% less time, yet 7.7% more cycles | Designed the gb ruler: before every timing, run a burst of 10 image-library bf16 GEMMs; proxy/prod are scored under gb. Not installed yet; requires user approval. No single op ruler can represent training | `1002__oe/RULER.md` §0–§1; `1002__e2e/RESULT-realab.md`; `1002__e2e/RESULT-e2e.md`; `0927__b0/profile/REPORT.md` §0 §2.1; `0930__roofline/REPORT.md` |
| In e2e, ASM is faster than either op ruler measures (unexplained) | A0-new-firmware 10-02: in e2e, ASM per layer takes fwd 1.07–1.11 ms and bwd 5.11–5.29 ms, shorter than the readings of both the blk and the gb ruler (realab, 6 layers of real data: fwd blk 1.209 / gb 1.137 ms, bwd blk 5.475 / gb 5.530 ms). B0 09-28 showed similar signs: ASM fwd does not change with sclk, but does change with conditions (iso 1.22, blk 1.45, eburst 1.13, layer 1.00 ms) | The clock cannot explain it. One possibility: in training, q/k/v have just been written by the preceding GEMM/RoPE and are still hot in MALL, whereas both rulers flush the cache before every call. Not measured | None; RULER §8.1 lists it as open | Between the op rulers and training there is another variable besides the clock, namely cache warmth; absolute ASM ms cannot be extrapolated directly to e2e | `1002__oe/RULER.md` §1 last item, §8.1; `1002__e2e/RESULT-realab.md`; `0927__b0/profile/REPORT.md` §2.1 |

In reality, ASM fwd was never called during training | `converters: []` kept turbo attention out of e2e entirely, so both sides were actually flex. The editable-installed primus_turbo in the image overrode PYTHONPATH via a `.pth` MetaPathFinder. `AITER_LOG_LEVEL=ERROR` suppressed the banner, so the criterion "no aiter in the log" was itself invalid | Inside the container, `pip uninstall -y primus_turbo` and print `__file__`; turn the converter on; have the gate write a trace file at the entry point | All 09-13/14 e2e conclusions about turbo attention are void (13,204 tps, 1.459×, "38% lever") | `0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:277-318`; `0914__campaign/HANDOFF.md` §4 |
| e2e statistical method | "ASM bwd makes training 6.78% slower (n=1)", "3.42% faster" and "OFF is very stable, 0.06%" were overturned one after another. MFU showed 178–249% | Step-to-step jitter within a single run was mistaken for cross-run variance; the random seed was not fixed; trimodality. torchtitan's `get_peak_flops` falls back to the A100's 312 TF/s for gfx1250 | Set `debug.seed 1234`; run alternating n≥9 with `bin/ab_replicate.sh`, then aggregate with `ab_summary.py`; steps 20 → 10 (medians over steps 4–8 differ by ≤0.22%); quote only tps/tflops | No MFU column can be quoted | `0915__opt/E2E-AB.md:180-260`; `0915__opt/GEMM-WGRAD-FINDING.md:78-94` |
| Contention on the cards | B0 09-14: with all four cards fully loaded, single-card timings drifted by up to 50% (11.2–17.1 ms), and false SQNR failures appeared. On A0, a meas2 left over from r23 occupied the card at the same time as the framework's validation for ~2 min | The whole board shares power and temperature, within the same hive; orphan processes | Use exclusive windows plus alternating measurement; never run sustained GEMM stress tests on any card; every entry in `rocm-smi --showpids` and every KFD holder must be identifiable one by one; when abandoning a session, kill by PID at the same time | — | `0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:84-97,217-233`; `0927__b0/interference.md`; 6029b430 |

The ledger's impl_note was hard-coded. A 9 s clock_probe window turned into ~55 min of card time. The harness rejected 0 as an illegal value. The subprocess's sys.path was wrong. impl_note was hard-coded before the run. enqueue had no back-pressure (372× over-issue). ~0.7 ms of that 0.9 ms was host-side overhead | Changed to `bash -lc 'cd "$0" && exec … "$@"'` and echo back the config actually received; flex uses max-autotune; added `_ZERO_MEANS_UNSET` (+4.2% at full clock); the ledger records turbo_path; audit with SQNR fingerprints (ASM dk 50.61 / dv 50.83, fused bwd 52.31 / 52.71); added a Throttle wrapper; when the two measurement methods disagree, treat the gap as not established | Several rows of ladder data had to be redone | `0914__repro__c07/RESULTS.md:81-113`; `0915__opt/LEDGER-AUDIT.md`; OE:`output/0911__fa_gfx1250_phase1/E1.md` §3 |

Early TF/s used the fwd+bwd total of 7.697e12. bwd has two ways of counting: "7-GEMM issued" and "5-GEMM nominal" | The old harness's fwd FLOP is ~2.233e12; `op_flops.py` uses fwd 2.199292e12 and bwd 5.498229e12 | Use `op_flops.py` throughout; give ms alongside when quoting; do not mix issued TF/s and algorithmic TF/s in comparisons | — | `0925__flydsl/fwd-job/NOTES.md:299-304`; skill:`baselines.md` §1 |

### d.4 Toolchain problems

| Problem | Symptom | Root cause | Solution | Cost/lesson | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Three FlyDSL versions coexist | The image ships 0.2.4 (Primus-Turbo main pins this version, for the gfx950 tree); 0.3.2 is the version pinned by aiter (`~/.local/flydsl032`, used by the bwd job); 0.3.4.1 lives in `~/.local/flydsl0341` (used by the fwd job and e2e). With 0.3.2 on PYTHONPATH, `import primus_turbo` fails with `cannot import name 'buffer_ops'`. The B0 smoke test reports `AssertionError: flydsl 0.2.4 @ /opt/venv` | 0.3.x removed `flydsl.expr.buffer_ops`. Primus's base_env puts the image's site-packages first, and `_ensure_flydsl0341()` does not move the path to the front once it sees it is already in sys.path. Installing matplotlib with pip --target let numpy 2.5.3 overwrite the 2.4.1 that torch depends on | `_env.py` does `sys.path.insert(0, dir)` before importing, and asserts the version and `__file__`; job processes never import primus_turbo. 0.3.2 and 0.3.4.1 perform on par in same-session A/B: fly/asm 0.657–0.664 and 0.664–0.666 respectively (A0-throttled); r29/s6 have byte-identical ISA under both versions | The Stage 4 dispatch wiring had to be redesigned. Cherry-picking 70607aa9 (compatibility patch) hit conflicts in 13 files and was aborted | skill:`env-and-pitfalls` §1a; `0917__flydsl/STAGE1-FWD.md` §4; `0925__flydsl/fwd341/ab_prod.log` |
| 0.2.4 is not capable enough (product port) | The 09-17 conclusion "0.2.4 plus 4 shims is enough" was overturned, with the error `state variable 'result' is list`. With hand-written intrinsics, 0.2.4's LLVM encoded the LDS operand as a global address, and the fault address was exactly the LDS offset. 0.2.4's rocm.py:61 passes wave64 for gfx1250. Under COMPILE_ONLY, 0.3.4.1's `flyc.compile` returns None; 0.2.4 does not have this early return | Added the compatibility layer `_flydsl_compat/common.py`. ### d.5 op-evolve framework issues

| Problem | Symptom | Root cause | Solution | Cost / lesson | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Precision gate set so strict that even the baseline failed it | The fwd 50 dB gate was failed even by the unmodified baseline: 7 edge cases at 49.82–49.99 dB. r1 longest-first was 8.6% faster on prod and was rejected because of this. In addition, `GATE_DB=50.0` was hard-coded in `op/ut/gates.py`. In the 0923 version of the fwd spec, `precision_gate` had been folded into the `refcache: >` block scalar and read back as None | The threshold was not calibrated against the baseline first; the same gate was defined in two places | Changed to 49 in the resolved yaml without bumping the spec; hand-edited `GATE_DB` to 49.0; fixed the spec | r1 rejected; r5–r10 recorded as passed=false in state, distorting the books | skill:`fwd.md:107,112`; skill:`op-evolve-ops` §10 |
| The UT gate was a no-op all along | pytest printed "no tests ran in 0.72s" | `seed=hash(shape)&0xFFFF` is salted by PYTHONHASHSEED, so it differs in every process and the cache can never hit; the file also contains no `test_` functions | 93ad3b89 changed it to seed=0; r11 is the first round in which the UT actually checked anything | — | 93ad3b89; `0923__flydsl/CAMPAIGN-FINAL.md` |
| Acceptance rule: arithmetic mean + min_gain 0 + fixed band | A0-throttled bwd r15 g47 was 3.1% slower on prod (0.9688), yet was promoted to champion on a gain of 1.0035 (propped up by fast). r5's 1.15× on the books was actually pulled up by fast; prod was only +1.6%. fwd r10 nodelay was +0.83% on prod in 4/4 runs, but only 1.0022 was left after averaging, and it was rejected. fwd r1's fast 0.934 was below the band 0.993 and vetoed the +8.6% on prod | acceptance averages the three shapes with equal weights; band = max(0.95, 1−min_gain), applied to every shape alike; min_gain is not a parameter that tune can change | 20baa45e rolled back the champion by hand; h7/h24 stipulate ranking by prod, and no ship when prod is below the round's floor; added `evolve.gain_weights {prod 1, proxy 0.25, fast 0}` and `shape_band` (fast 0.90, proxy 0.98); min_gain set to 0.007 | Changing the rules requires hand-editing final.yaml and then a plain resume | 20baa45e; skill:`op-evolve-ops` §5; skill:`fwd.md:101-112` |
| Champion records polluted by unpromoted arms | B0 fwd r9 BG beat r6 on all three shapes, yet scored 0.9984 and was rejected. r19 was +1.5% on the real dumps but was blocked by the record of an unreleased r18 arm (0.9998). In bwd champions, fast/proxy=30 are records from the rejected r30 | The per-shape best-ever is updated even in rejected rounds | The operator promoted by hand via refactor hints (h39 → r10, h44, h48/h68); recommendation: champions should record only promoted rounds | — | `0927__b0/OP-EVOLVE-SUGGESTIONS.md` #1 |
| target_met decided by geometric mean | B0 bwd r27: fast was 1.66× faster than beat (ASM is slow on small shapes), masking a ~0.78× deficit on prod/proxy; geomean 1.007, and the job ended on its own | The target criterion is the geometric mean over the three shapes | With user approval, changed to h69: proxy and prod must each be ≥ the same-process beat; fast is report-only. Later, on A0, `BEAT_MARGIN=20` (target 1.20×) was also set, so that the job does not end at the first round that meets the target | — | `0927__b0/bwd-hint.md` h69; `0930__bwd/oejob/README.md` |
| Hints never delivered | fwd hints were numbered L#/f#, but `core/hints.py` only parses table rows whose first cell is `h<N>`, so not a single one was read; moreover, hint.md was only put in place after r1 had started. The profiling/plan/act steps of deep rounds do not read hint.md at all (ae6dd218). The deep_loop-trim patch (OE 58b2134) hard-coded the bwd-only CAMPAIGN CORRECTIONS into 3 preambles, so the fwd deep rounds (A0 r5/r10, B0 r15/r20) all read bwd content. h83 had only paragraphs and no index row, and was not in effect from 09-30 to 10-02. `core/route.py` matches the type against the whole row, so "must be predicated" in the condition column was taken as must, and fwd r1 raised a RouteError as a result | The parser only recognizes the index table; the deep modules do not import hints; the preambles are shared by all jobs | Switched to a four-column index table (id, type, title, status); every hint must have an index row, self-checked with `core/hints.py`; route.py reads only the type column; prepared a fwd version of the deep prompt patch and a switch-over script | It cannot be confirmed which rounds actually read which rules | skill:`fwd.md:34-35,106-109`; s:69864fc9 10-02T11:41Z; `1002__oe/FWDJOB.md` |
| Deep rounds "flying blind" | h4 (09-22) banned profiling and deep rounds on the grounds that APERTURE_VIOLATION appeared under rocprofv3; on 09-23, S0-c established that this was a confound caused by eager Tensile. The stock 01_select forces `--kernel-trace` and got 0 rows. PC sampling in the r17 deep round faulted on prod. deep act allows only one candidate | The ban rested on confounded evidence; the framework's default profiler usage does not work on gfx1250 | af33a8fd enabled deep rounds (ATT tested); 82420bdf banned PC sampling; on 09-30, after the reflash, ATT was wired into 04_thread_trace; fast rounds switched to PMC csv (h80) | — | af33a8fd; 82420bdf; `0930__bwd/REPORT.md:35` |
| `resume --config` rebuilds everything | `apply_spec` bumps spec_version, causing both setup stages to rerun (~58 min; op_setup measured 2850 s) and regenerating `op/{baseline,eager,ut,benchmark.py,validation.py}`, which wipes out the refcache and all hand patches. min_gain is not a tune option; tune does not modify state.yaml. bwd's `validation.py` hard-codes `BEAT_MARGIN=0.0`, so `tune --beat-margin` changed only the text, not the gate | This is how the framework is designed | Use only `op-evolve tune`, or first back up final.yaml (`.bak.<reason>`), then hand-edit it, then do a plain `resume` | With this approach, the bwd spec was still v000 after three hand edits | skill:`op-evolve-ops` §4 |
| refcache vs. computing references on the card | The gate, benchmark, check_determinism, bitwise.py and test_correctness.py, 5 places in all, ran the fp32 Tensile reference on the card; this is the source of class-A wedges. From B0 r24 on, changing a single toy-shape line in `ut/common.py` was enough to break the common_sha match, so every round the gate printed "IGNORED … recomputing" and recomputed the prod reference on the card (exactly the trigger path of the 09-22 AC power cycle); the same happened on A0 on 09-30. A script written by the B0 fwd r20 act itself also ran an fp32 reference on the card, and GPU2 faulted as a result | Reference computation was designed to run on the GPU; on a sha mismatch it silently falls back to recomputing | 376b9fd3, 00128018, a5693e9c and 93ad3b89 moved all call sites to the refcache (prod reference computed on the CPU, 10m26s, 1.1 GB). B0 changed the provenance from 988c14ca to d441e55a; A0 accepts the recorded sha. A cache miss is an immediate FAIL. h50 bans computing fp32 references on the card | On 09-21..22 that path consumed 82% of the wall-clock time | 376b9fd3; `0927__b0/bwd-hint.md` h69; `0930__bwd/oejob/README.md` |
| Process management and supervision | The VENV in `supervise_job.sh` was hard-coded to someone else's path; when the bin was not found it exited 127 and then restarted forever. MAX_RESTARTS=60, and the backoff only grows, never shrinks. The supervisor only checks whether the process has died, not for wedges or stalls. Killing the loop with `kill <pid>` leaves orphaned agents that keep writing job_context. An act interrupted by `op-evolve stop` reruns from scratch next time (B0 bwd r25 ran twice because of this). Before stop takes effect, the framework has already built the empty shell of the next round. The 09-25 resume did not use setsid and kept its logs in /tmp; they were lost after the 10:10:51 reboot. meas2 left over from r23 occupied the card concurrently with validation. On 09-24 the `.stop` came a few tens of seconds late, and the next round's opt had already started | The framework monitors only one dimension, "process death"; manual operating habits were wrong | Use `setsid nohup … resume`; stop with `op-evolve stop` or `kill -TERM -<PGID>`; deploy card_ok.sh, patrol and release_guard; in monitoring, use a bracket pattern such as `[o]p-evolve` to avoid matching itself | — | skill:`op-evolve-ops` §3 §11; `0917__flydsl/op-evolve/LAUNCHED.md` |
| Ledger and state defects | The flops key in state.yaml is misaligned (A0 from r18 on, B0 from r20 on), so the ms column in progress.md is wrong; the TF/s itself is correct. After a shape rename, champions forked (A0 r20 had both `prod:19` and the long-name `:20`). A refactor clears all champions. After a refactor the same round still reuses `rounds/N/op`: the fwd job's `rounds/016/op` is actually the rejected g61, and r17–19 all re-measured g61 as the champion. FastError: if one idea was not written into facts/dead_ends, the whole round was recorded as failed (A0 r22, r26). setup does not report the speed of round 0 | Framework implementation defects | Always derive ms as FLOP ÷ TF/s; use only the three names fast/proxy/prod in act.yaml (h35); measure the incumbent with `--arms current` (h51); before state_edit.py rebases, first verify that `rounds/<best>/op` and `op/current` are byte-identical | A root fix requires changing the framework, which needs user approval; not done this time | `1002__oe/RULER.md` §3.3–3.4; `0923__flydsl/hint.md` h35 |
| Multi-card scheduling and multiple code trees | gpu_id was written only into the prompt, and the gate's docker exec carried no device variable, so it always ran on device 0. preflight requires the whole machine to be idle, so power_wall was always skipped. On 09-14 multiple vendored primus_turbo trees were loaded in the same process: the sys.modules guard fired; the opaque-type registry is process-global; both trees declare `primus_turbo::attention_triton_forward_impl`, and when the second torch.library fragment is destroyed it pulls the schema out from under the first one's live handle (use-after-free), showing up as intermittent internal assertions, bad_alloc and SIGSEGV 139 | The framework assumes a single card and a single tree by default | On B0, one container per card (snapshotted with docker commit, mounting only /dev/kfd and one renderD); modules switched to the private prefix `_op_evolve_<n>.`, with ops registered under `primus_turbo_opevolve::`; one process per arm | The use-after-free ate 4 validation runs, about 2 h | `0927__b0/README.md`; `0927__b0/OP-EVOLVE-SUGGESTIONS.md` #4 #6; `0914__campaign/op-evolve-artifacts/…/dead_ends.md` |
| Agent infrastructure and safety | The CLI bundled with claude-agent-sdk 0.2.152 in `.venv` does not support claude-opus-5-5. B0 had no `~/.op_evolve_openai`, so the codex reviewer returned 401 and the deep round failed. `runtime.env` in the spec is silently ineffective. review auto-approves after 10 min. The opt agent wrote its own probe and ran on prod a kernel that had never been on the card, causing the 10-02 wedge | Unsuitable dependency versions, account configuration and framework defaults; the agent did not know the card-safety rules | Upgraded to claude-agent-sdk ≥0.2.159; switched the reviewer to claude; environment variables written into the op code; h85 written into the hint table and into the deep round's CAMPAIGN CORRECTIONS | The agent's permissions have to be constrained by hints; a rule not written as a table row is as good as not written | skill:`fwd.md:110`; `0927__b0/HANDOFF-A0.md` §3; 1ee0dd59 |

### d.6 Correctness and precision gates

| Problem | Symptom | Root cause | Solution | Cost / lesson | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A four-tensor SQNR gate is needed | On 09-13 (A0-throttled), dk/dv = −inf occurred once, while out 53.67 and dq 52.24 dB were both normal (the run at the time was `fwd:num_stages=2; bwd:num_warps=2,waves_per_eu=1`). This was a one-off event: about 1/100 of runs; 3 immediate repeats all passed, and it did not recur in the following 2,300 calls (1000+1000 in-process, 300 in total across 150 fresh processes); the original write-up judged it "not a property of any config" and suspected a transient hardware fault; but it shows that a gate looking only at out/dq misses errors. In aiter, changing only `BLOCK_N1=256` was 1.31× faster, but dq was only 9.59 dB. With dkdv's BLOCK_M changed to 128, the result was "smoothly wrong". When num_block_m did not follow BLOCK_M, the dk/dv tail stayed at the zeros initial value. `sequence_parallel=False` computed only 1/128 | A gate that looks only at out cannot see backward errors; the grid and the tiling parameters are coupled to each other | out/dq/dk/dv each ≥50 dB; before dispatch, assert `BLOCK_N1==BLOCK_M2` and `BLOCK_M1==BLOCK_N2` (rejected 3 configs); prefill outputs with NaN and check isfinite coverage; the fwd gate imposes separate requirements on o and lse (later lowered to 49 dB, see d.5) | Wrong configs often look faster | `0913__opt_plan__claude/phase1/RESULTS.md` §4 §12 (:89-95, :380-393); `0913__opt_plan__claude/phase2/DECISIONS.md` D1 D4 D8 |
| The determinism test measured its own ruler | On 09-13 the default config was run 25 times; dk showed 3 different SQNR values and was reported as "dk non-deterministic" | The fp32 reference was recomputed every time, and the rocBLAS path the reference takes is itself non-deterministic | Switched to bitwise comparison against the result of run 0 (`--determinism-reps`, 0.47 s per run, previously 30 s); the champion and the factory default each ran 1000 times, then each ran two rounds in 150 fresh processes, 0 mismatches | Do not test determinism against a freshly exported reference | `0913__opt_plan__claude/phase1/RESULTS.md` §4 §12 |
| Splitting the determinism gate: dk/dv bitwise, dq by SQNR | The spec says "introducing split-k or atomics means failure", yet validation.py only does 200 bitwise comparisons and has no means of detecting split-k; fixed-order split-k had been shipping every round since r13. check_determinism runs only one shape, fast. aiter ASM's dq is itself an fp32 atomic accumulation (514 `buffer_atomic_add_f32` instructions) and cannot pass our own gate; the structural gap of 7-GEMM vs. 5-GEMM (1.386–1.408×) therefore could not be narrowed under the old rule | The intent of the rule is "each output element is written only once"; 200 bitwise runs are only a cheap way to observe it | 09-25 c741fa8d: dk/dv keep 200-run bitwise consistency; dq changed to SQNR ≥70 dB between repeated runs on the same input, calibrated with aiter's measured dq (fast 113.0 / prod 98.0 dB). The champion passes without code changes | Leftover: the determinism test still needs to be extended to (q_split, BLOCK_KV) × {fast, prod} | c741fa8d; `0925__flydsl/PARITY-STRATEGY.md` R0; `0925__flydsl/AITER-5GEMM-STUDY.md` §5 |
| `bench_attention_turbo` has a buggy reference | Running the product-branch bench on 09-30: under non-causal s8192, dq for every backend was only 12.0–12.4 dB and was judged FAIL (triton rows: out 60.6, dk 61.0; flydsl rows: out 48.8, dk 50.0) | The benchmark's built-in reference is inaccurate at this shape; measured against a CPU fp32 reference it is 52 dB | Recorded in memory, not fixed; the PR states that this is not a kernel error | Easy to misjudge as a product-kernel bug | `0930__port/runs/bench_turbo_triton.log`; `0930__port/runs/bench_turbo_flydsl.log`; s:fdc2534d 09-30T05:29Z |
| Poisoning allocator (poison allocator) | A candidate's out-of-bounds write clobbered the interrupt ring, yet poison_allocator did not catch the "never-written elements" | The largest poison block was only 64 MiB while prod's dq is 256 MiB, so the caching allocator never hands a poison block to dq, and fresh pages are all 0. The fill value was fp32 NaN 0x7FC00000, half of which reads as 0.0 when read as bf16. In our own fix, 0x7FC07FC0−2^32 in turn overflowed int32. In addition, the offline table's mean-SQNR gate lets NaN through (`nan < 50` is False) | Switched to 0x7FC07FC0 (NaN in all three dtypes), written through an int32 view, with block sizes covering downward from the largest tensor; placed in `op/poison_util.py` (686c9927) without touching ut/common.py, so that the refcache is not invalidated; prefill with NaN and check isfinite coverage (720 calls, 0 non-finite values) | — | cba2abc2; 686c9927; `0923__flydsl/poison-fix/README.md`; `0915__opt/NAN-FINDING.md:66-73` |
| Contention causing false SQNR failures | On B0 on 09-14, while an orphaned sweep process was grabbing the card: llama31-8b-s4096 fused out 43.23, gate-s2048 42.88, gate-s1024 asm 48.48 (threshold 50). Re-measured in an exclusive window: 53.74/53.87/53.93 | A shared card | Any SQNR failure only counts after it has been reproduced once in an exclusive window | Correct configs were nearly killed by mistake | `0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:217-233`; skill:`gfx1250-card-safety` §7 |
| Thin SQNR margin, low discriminating power | The SQNR ceiling of bf16 gradients is about 52–53 dB, only about 2.5 dB above the 50 dB gate. ASM bwd's dk/dv are only 50.2–51.0 dB (dk 50.56, dv 50.84 at s4096), because the host side first rounds each of the g=4 partial sums to bf16 and then adds them. armN changed the fp32 accumulation order, yet SQNR still read 52.61/52.65/52.83, showing no difference. In the region skipped by causal masking the true values are close to 0, so an off-by-one or a race could still pass the gate | SQNR is a global average | Rely mainly on element-wise bitwise comparison: armAB vs. the incumbent had zero differences across 134,217,728 elements. SQNR is only auxiliary; use SQNR fingerprints to audit which implementation actually ran | When the threshold is raised or the shape changes, ASM will be the first to hit the line | `0922_summary` (xlsx NOTES 4/12); `0925__flydsl/AITER-5GEMM-STUDY.md` §2 |
| Finding out-of-bounds accesses by proof | On 09-24 an exhaustive enumeration of prod's 134,217,728 tuples proved that the reads and writes of k_dq and k_dq_sp are all bijections onto the real tensors. It also found 3 latent defects: (1) impl.py:115 asserts only sq%32, while the launcher rounds down and the kernel rounds up, so when sq%64==32 there is an out-of-bounds write of 262,144 B; (2) dqp's num_records is computed in int32 and wraps to 0 when nsp≥8, so all dQ writes are dropped; (3) k_dkdv's prefetch is not clamped and reads up to 982,272 B past the end, stopped only by the real extent. B0 r19 made 1,580 out-of-bounds prefetches across 9 UT shapes (256 on prod) | Address-arithmetic changes came without matching bounds proofs | On B0, the three fixes from h33 gave r19h: 0 out-of-bounds accesses, bitwise-identical output, no measurable overhead (1.0001). w4f was re-proved after its proof model was corrected (it had mistaken a partial wait for retirement of the whole TDM batch). Note: do not change k_dq's descriptor to the real extent; that would drop 75% of dQ | — | 41c916b7; `0924__flydsl/kdq-enumeration/SYNTHESIS.md`; `0927__b0/lab-bwd-r19/REPORT.md` §3–§4 |
| Gates for the product port | Regression tests found that the gate let G=6/7 through, and fwd asserted at build time; at G=32, n_seq=0. An adversarial review confirmed 3 more issues: the window check on the apply path, the autotune cache key, and the launch memo under COMPILE_ONLY | fwd's O store requires G to divide 32 and be ≤16 | Gate tightened to G∈{1,2,4,8,16}; added a gqa16 precision test and rejection tests for G=7/32; committed only after the 3 review issues were fixed | — | s:fdc2534d 09-30T04:37–04:56Z |
| e2e correctness: the NaN runs were actually the fastest | 8 of 74 e2e runs had NaN loss, and each was the fastest in its group: fly 49,487 vs. 46.5k (+6.4%), asm-on 44,294 vs. 42.5k, v3 38,713 vs. 37.7k, nkfix2-on 12,101 vs. 11,650 | Scoring by tps only, never looking at loss; broken computation is often faster | `e2e.sh` counts nan_steps and prints DISCARD; judge correctness first, then speed; if one run goes wrong, re-audit all runs; the watchdog in later e2e runs exits on any non-finite value (exit 96) | 4 already-reported numbers were retracted (see d.2) | `PROGRESS-REPORT-0917.html`; skill:`gfx1250-card-safety` §8; `1002__e2e/E2E-PLAN.md` |

For ASM's own correctness problems, see section (b): out-of-bounds dk/dv writes under GQA (−0.94/−0.65 dB; the workaround is `dkdv_heads=q` plus a host-side reduction), dq of `_perf.co` only 5.84 dB, and ASM fwd's LSE layout is incompatible with the two-kernel bwd (mixing them silently computes wrong gradients).

The AC power cycle on the night of 09-22: 255 objects truncated, the branch ref pointing to the corrupted 70efc407 (unrecoverable); ROUND3-CLOSEOUT.md was also truncated at 16384 B (2277 trailing NULs), and this corrupted state had even been committed once. At the time, 4 commits had never been pushed | A commit was half-written when the power went out | First quarantine the 0-byte objects, then `git update-ref <branch> <last-good-commit>`, then `rm .git/index && git reset`, and finally `git fsck --no-dangling`. Since 09-23 07:36 the rule is that every round must end with a push (memory `push-after-every-round`); run fsck after every AC power cycle; save changes to the op-evolve repo separately as patches | Lost 2 commits (contents still in the working tree) and the tails of several files. The skill records the first incident as "2026-09-16 cycle"; per the session records it was actually 09-17 | s:c4b79aa6 09-17T08:02Z; s:a9a96fef 09-23T07:13–07:29Z; 43eb5d89; f940fac6 |

On 09-21 the user added a Bash permission rule in `~/.claude/settings.json`, and the push succeeded at 09:22 | 4 days without an off-site backup | s:b596bddb 09-15T03:18Z; s:c4b79aa6 09-17T13:25Z, 09-21T09:22Z |

On 10-02 07:52–07:55 the real dumps were extracted from it to `/home/lihuzhan/_prof_dump` (sha256 verified), and the same day realab used them for make-up measurements on A0 (see d.3); the fwd job was also restored from this archive. The B0 bwd job directory has still not been unpacked | s:b596bddb 09-15T02:20–04:07Z; `0927__b0/HANDOFF-A0.md`; `0928__bak_b0/MANIFEST.md` §2–§4; `0930__bwd/PLAN.md:16,20`; `0930__bwd/REPORT.md:16`; s:69864fc9 10-02T07:51–07:55Z; `1002__oe/FWDJOB.md:11`; `1002__e2e/RESULT-realab.md:3` |

#### Open or unverified items (section d scope)

- The direct causes of class-B wedges (starting with INVALIDATE_TLBS) and of the startup-time MES fault family are both still unknown. There is also no usable precursor indicator.
- The root cause of nkfix producing NaN in 21% of runs on A0 has not been found. Between the host hipBLASLt library and NaN and wedges there is only correlational evidence, apart from the single-variable pair B0 p1a/p1b (n=1 per side; it answers the objection raised in B0 VERIFY); this has not been confirmed repeatedly on the card.
- The 09-21 16:01:54 wedge was judged unrecoverable by the session, but 4547915c says dmesg had no reset signature at all. The first-line signature of the 09-22 16:02 wedge was not saved. Neither of the two can be assigned a class.
- In the AC count, the two on 09-11 (AC power cycle or warm reboot) and the 9th on 09-16 (inferred) are uncertain. The reboot times on 09-13 and 10-02 were not recorded.
- Documents that need correcting:
  - `env-and-pitfalls` §1b still recommends the host library, which conflicts with h85 and the tutorial.
  - `gfx1250-card-safety` says "five AC power cycles in one day on 09-16"; it was actually 9.
  - The same skill says "the 09-16 cycle truncated 6 objects"; it was actually 09-17.
  - skill:`gfx1250-attn-campaign/SKILL.md:106` and `0930__bwd/PLAN.md` D2 say PC sampling "wedged 3/3"; the original record is that all 3 faulted and 1 wedged (the "3 attempts 3 faults" in `env-and-pitfalls` §2c is accurate).
  - `0930__bwd/PLAN.md` D7 and bwd hint h83 say A0 r24 was accepted by "geometric mean"; it was actually the arithmetic mean (20baa45e).
  - `0930__bwd/PLAN.md` P4/D1 says the r24–r32 history of the B0 bwd job is unobtainable, and `0930__bwd/REPORT.md:16` says the real dumps exist only on B0; in fact both have been on A0 since 09-28, in `/home/lihuzhan/code/0928__bak_b0.tar.gz` (see d.7).
  - The 1ee0dd59 commit message, `1002__oe/incident/WEDGE-1002.md`, the body of h85 (`0930__bwd/oejob/hint.md:4806`) and `gfx1250-card-safety` §1 #13 record the two variants of the 10-02 r27 wedge as w4f fused kernels; per the source in `rounds/027/_scratch/arms`, they were actually cluster multicast on an s6 base (A_g74 modifies k_dkdv, B_g82 modifies k_dqg), see d.1.1.
- The gb ruler has been designed but not installed (needs user approval; optionally spend ~6 min of card time first to calibrate the burst length). The magnitude of s6 vs. ASM was measured on 10-02 (A0-new-firmware, real data): blk 0.972, gb 1.038, and an in-e2e attention bwd per-step time ratio of 0.974 (p1: 162.15 / 166.54 ms; this is the bwd time ratio, not the whole-step ratio. bwd is ~4 ms faster than ASM per step: adjacent-pair differences 4.50 / 4.56 ms, i.e. the 4.5 in the main text of `1002__e2e/RESULT-e2e.md`; subtracting the two arms' medians gives 3.8–4.4 ms), so gb is pessimistic by ~6% on this item.
- In the same e2e, fly's attention total (fwd+bwd) per step was ~4.3–5.2 ms more than ASM's (fwd 43.9 vs. 34.7, +9.2; bwd ~−4), yet the whole step was 0.7–2.0 ms faster (1,349.4 / 1,349.9 vs. 1,350.6). In both processes step − FA is −6.3 ms, beyond the 5 ms threshold of criterion 2 in E2E-PLAN §4; the same holds for s6 vs. r29 (attention −53.0 ms, while the step is only 37.0–39.1 ms faster). The difference comes from parts outside attention or from noise, and has not been broken down [`1002__e2e/e2e/runs/analysis.1002_095504.txt`].
- In e2e, ASM per layer (fwd 1.07–1.11 ms, bwd 5.11–5.29 ms) is shorter than what either of the two op rulers measures, which the clock cannot explain; the hypothesis that "the inputs are hot in MALL" was not measured (RULER §8.1).
- Two documents disagree on L21's real gain: REPORT-0928.html says +0.3%, while the blocked reading in ruler/REPORT.md is +0.6%.

## Appendix A Conventions and comparability

### A.1 Machines, clock stages and driver/firmware

The four sections name the same stage differently: (a) uses A0-VR / A0-RF / B0, (b) uses E1–E5, (c) uses A0-H / A0-T / B0-14 / B0-28 / A0-R / A0-D, and (d) uses A0-throttled / A0-new-firmware / B0. The mapping is as follows.

| Stage | Labels used by each section | Machine | Date (UTC) | VBIOS / SMU / driver | Clock under load | Comparability and notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A0 unthrottled | (c) A0-H | A0 `heliosr-1b114-c07-1`, dedicated 1× gfx1250 (MI455X, 256 CU, wave32, 320 KB LDS per CU, 432 GiB HBM, PPT0 power cap 2500 W) | 09-04 and earlier | Not recorded separately | 1699–1703 MHz under load on 09-04 (cap 2400) | This project has no attention measurements from this stage |
| A0 VR-throttled | (a) A0-VR; (b) E1 (09-10..09-17), E3 (09-17..09-27), E3′ (09-28); (c) A0-T; (d) A0-throttled | A0 | 09-10..09-28 (the earliest throttle record is 09-10 22:38) | VBIOS `113-M4500001-630A`; SMU fw 125.7.1; amdgpu 7.1.1.31300009 (dkms 7.1.1-2397345); kernel 6.14.0-37-generic, cmdline with `modprobe.blacklist=amdgpu` | sclk DPM left with only 500/1100 MHz; ~0.94–1.07 GHz under load (998–1029 MHz measured in the 09-24 prod timing window) | After 09-29, all absolute numbers from this stage are void; only same-process ratios may be quoted |
| B0 09-14 | (b) E2; (c) B0-14 | B0 `ctheliosp-1b112-a37-1`, 4× gfx1250 (GPU0 degraded; only GPU1–3 used that day) | 09-14 | Not recorded (same image as A0) | 2133–2244 MHz | With all four cards under load at once, per-card timing drifted by up to 50% |
| B0 09-27/28 | (b) E4; (c) B0-28; (d) B0 | B0, one snapshot container per card, fa-g0..g3; GPU1 wedged for good on 09-28 and had still not recovered at hand-back | 09-27..09-28 | Not recorded | Idle 2356 MHz; op level (blocked ruler) ~2.15 GHz; whole training step after nkfix ~1.34–1.39 GHz (final runs 1,342 / 1,371 MHz), attention window during training 1.25–1.69 GHz (board-level power limit ~2.13 kW) | The B0 09-27/28 sessions are not on this machine; the numbers come only from documents and ledgers |
| A0 after reflash | (a) A0-RF; (b) E5; (c) A0-R; (d) A0-new-firmware | A0 | 09-29..10-01 | VBIOS 700E; SMU 125.12.0; amdgpu-dkms 7.1.0-2411946 | sclk DPM 500/2356/2400; op level 1.74–2.03 GHz; full-load WMMA ~1.4–1.8 GHz (2500 W power limit); ~1.5 GHz during training | VR warnings are still printed at boot, but DPM is no longer clipped to 1100 MHz; ATT works |
| A0 new driver | (b) E5 (from 10-01); (c) A0-D; (d) A0-new-firmware | A0 | From 10-01 | VBIOS 700E unchanged; amdgpu-dkms 7.1.0-2412954 | Same as above; ~1.28 GHz after a GEMM burst (training operating point); e2e whole-step sclk median ~1.50 GHz | randn baseline consistent with 09-30 (bwd s6 at 104.1% of ASM, fwd time ratio 1.079) |

**Software stack**: container `fa-repro` (image `fa-tune:deps`, i.e. `amdprimus/amdprimus:gfx1250-20260910` plus the torchtitan dependencies), torch `2.11.0+rocm7.14.0a20260625`, triton 3.6.0, HIP 7.14, python 3.12.3; aiter-src was @`ffa945f9` on 09-13 and @`6963ae9d` from 09-17 on; torchtitan v0.2.2 (`73a0e6979`). Three flydsl versions coexist:
- 0.2.4: ships with the image; Primus-Turbo main and the product branch are pinned to it;
- 0.3.2: the version pinned by aiter (`~/.local/flydsl032`); used by the A0 bwd job and the hand-campaign s1–s6;
- 0.3.4.1: `~/.local/flydsl0341`; used by the fwd job, B0 and e2e from 09-25 on.
0.3.x removed `flydsl.expr.buffer_ops`, so it cannot be used in the same process as primus_turbo.

Of the three items only the fwd 1.118× in the row above holds | `0915__opt/JIRA-TRACE-ANALYSIS.md` section five; (a) Notation and conventions ②; (b) b.4, last row; (d) d.3 |
